### Question 1: Acute Pancreatitis & Eicosanoid Synthesis
- **Difficulty**: Medium
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Acute pancreatitis involves cellular injury leading to the release of intracellular calcium, which activates cytosolic phospholipase A2 (cPLA2). cPLA2 hydrolyzes membrane phospholipids, releasing arachidonic acid, the precursor for eicosanoid synthesis. (急性胰腺炎涉及细胞损伤导致细胞内钙释放，激活胞浆磷脂酶A2 (cPLA2)。cPLA2水解膜磷脂，释放花生四烯酸，它是类二十碳烯酸合成的前体。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 38-year-old male presents to the emergency department with severe epigastric pain radiating to his back, nausea, and vomiting. He reports heavy alcohol consumption over the past several years. Physical examination reveals abdominal tenderness, guarding, and rebound tenderness in the epigastric region. His vital signs show a heart rate of 110 bpm, blood pressure of 90/60 mmHg, and respiratory rate of 24 breaths/min. Laboratory tests show elevated serum amylase and lipase levels (3x upper limit of normal), leukocytosis, and evidence of systemic inflammatory response syndrome (SIRS). A CT scan confirms acute pancreatitis with peripancreatic fluid collections. During the acute inflammatory phase, pancreatic acinar cells undergo significant cellular injury. Which of the following enzymatic events is the *initial* and *rate-limiting* step in the synthesis of prostaglandins and leukotrienes from membrane phospholipids in this patient's inflamed pancreatic tissue?

(A) Activation of cyclooxygenase (COX) enzymes by arachidonic acid.
(B) Release of lysophospholipids from the plasma membrane.
(C) Hydrolysis of sn-1 acyl ester bonds in membrane glycerophospholipids by phospholipase A1 (PLA1).
(D) Hydrolysis of sn-2 acyl ester bonds in membrane glycerophospholipids by cytosolic phospholipase A2 (cPLA2), releasing arachidonic acid.
(E) Conversion of arachidonic acid to 5-hydroxyeicosatetraenoic acid (5-HETE) by 5-lipoxygenase (5-LOX).

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 38yo male, heavy alcohol use, severe epigastric pain radiating to back, nausea, vomiting, abdominal tenderness/guarding/rebound, tachycardia, hypotension, tachypnea. This strongly suggests acute pancreatitis, likely alcohol-induced.
- **Lab/Imaging Findings:** Elevated amylase/lipase, leukocytosis, SIRS, CT scan confirming acute pancreatitis. These confirm the diagnosis and indicate significant inflammation.
- **Pathophysiology Focus:** The question asks about the *initial* and *rate-limiting* step in eicosanoid synthesis (prostaglandins, leukotrienes) during the *acute inflammatory phase* in *inflamed pancreatic tissue*. This points towards the biochemical cascade triggered by cellular injury.
- **Cellular Injury & Inflammation:** Acute pancreatitis involves acinar cell injury and necrosis. This injury leads to the release of intracellular components and triggers inflammatory signaling pathways.
- **Calcium Release:** Cellular injury often causes an influx of calcium ions (Ca2+) into the cytosol.
- **cPLA2 Activation:** Increased cytosolic Ca2+ is a key activator of cytosolic phospholipase A2 (cPLA2).
- **Arachidonic Acid Release:** Activated cPLA2 cleaves the sn-2 position of membrane glycerophospholipids (like phosphatidylcholine), releasing free arachidonic acid (20:4n-6).
- **Eicosanoid Synthesis:** Arachidonic acid is the substrate for cyclooxygenases (COX) to produce prostaglandins and thromboxanes, and for lipoxygenases (LOX) to produce leukotrienes and lipoxins.
- **Rate-Limiting Step:** The release of arachidonic acid from membrane phospholipids by cPLA2 is considered the rate-limiting step in this pathway.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
1.  **Initiating Event:** Acute pancreatitis causes pancreatic acinar cell injury and necrosis.
2.  **Calcium Influx:** Cellular damage leads to disruption of cellular homeostasis, including increased intracellular calcium concentration ([Ca2+]i).
3.  **cPLA2 Activation:** Elevated [Ca2+]i binds to calcium-binding sites on cytosolic phospholipase A2 (cPLA2), activating the enzyme.
4.  **Phospholipid Hydrolysis:** Activated cPLA2 targets membrane glycerophospholipids, primarily phosphatidylcholine. It specifically hydrolyzes the ester bond at the sn-2 position (the position occupied by arachidonic acid).
5.  **Arachidonic Acid Release:** This hydrolysis releases free arachidonic acid (20:4n-6) from the membrane phospholipid and generates lysophospholipids (e.g., lysophosphatidylcholine).
6.  **Eicosanoid Synthesis:** The released arachidonic acid is then metabolized by various enzymes:
    *   **Cyclooxygenases (COX-1, COX-2):** Convert arachidonic acid into prostaglandin H2 (PGH2), which is further metabolized into prostaglandins (PGE2, PGI2, PGF2α) and thromboxanes (TXA2).
    *   **Lipoxygenases (5-LOX, 12-LOX, 15-LOX):** Convert arachidonic acid into hydroperoxyeicosatetraenoic acids (HPETEs), which are further metabolized into leukotrienes (LTB4, LTC4, LTD4, LTE4) and lipoxins.
7.  **Rate-Limiting Step:** The availability of free arachidonic acid is crucial for eicosanoid synthesis. The release of arachidonic acid from membrane phospholipids by cPLA2 is the initial step and is tightly regulated, making it the rate-limiting step in the overall pathway.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): **Activation of cyclooxygenase (COX) enzymes by arachidonic acid.** This is a *downstream* step in prostaglandin synthesis. Arachidonic acid must first be released from the membrane before COX can act upon it. Therefore, it is not the *initial* or *rate-limiting* step for the overall pathway.
- (B): **Release of lysophospholipids from the plasma membrane.** While the hydrolysis of phospholipids by cPLA2 *does* produce lysophospholipids, this is a *product* of the reaction, not the primary event being asked about (the release of the substrate for eicosanoids). The key event is the release of arachidonic acid.
- (C): **Hydrolysis of sn-1 acyl ester bonds in membrane glycerophospholipids by phospholipase A1 (PLA1).** PLA1 hydrolyzes the sn-1 position, releasing fatty acids like oleic acid or palmitic acid, not arachidonic acid (which is typically at the sn-2 position). Furthermore, PLA1 is generally associated with the release of fatty acids from lipoproteins or digestion, not the primary inflammatory pathway involving arachidonic acid release in response to cellular injury. cPLA2 is the key enzyme for releasing arachidonic acid in this context.
- (E): **Conversion of arachidonic acid to 5-hydroxyeicosatetraenoic acid (5-HETE) by 5-lipoxygenase (5-LOX).** This is a specific step in the leukotriene synthesis pathway, occurring *after* arachidonic acid has been released. It is not the initial or rate-limiting step for eicosanoid synthesis in general.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The initial and rate-limiting step in the synthesis of prostaglandins and leukotrienes from membrane phospholipids during inflammation is the release of arachidonic acid via hydrolysis of the sn-2 ester bond by cytosolic phospholipase A2 (cPLA2), which is activated by increased intracellular calcium. Key differentials to consider include the downstream enzymes (COX, LOX) and other phospholipases (PLA1, PLA2G). Remember that arachidonic acid is the common precursor for both prostaglandin and leukotriene pathways.

### Question 2: Rheumatoid Arthritis Flare & Glucocorticoid Mechanism
- **Difficulty**: Medium
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Glucocorticoids exert rapid anti-inflammatory effects by inducing the synthesis of Annexin A1 (Lipocortin-1), which inhibits phospholipase A2 (PLA2), thereby blocking the production of arachidonic acid and its downstream inflammatory mediators (prostaglandins, leukotrienes). (糖皮质激素通过诱导 Annexin A1 (Lipocortin-1) 的合成来发挥快速抗炎作用，Annexin A1 抑制磷脂酶 A2 (PLA2)，从而阻断花生四烯酸及其下游炎症介质（前列腺素、白三烯）的产生。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 45-year-old female with a known history of severe rheumatoid arthritis (RA) presents to the emergency department experiencing an acute flare characterized by intense joint pain, swelling, and stiffness, particularly in her hands and wrists. Her C-reactive protein (CRP) level is markedly elevated at 120 mg/L (normal < 10 mg/L), and her erythrocyte sedimentation rate (ESR) is 95 mm/hr (normal < 20 mm/hr). Physical examination reveals significant tenderness, warmth, and effusion in multiple small joints. The patient is started on a course of oral dexamethasone 4 mg twice daily. Within 24 hours, her subjective joint pain significantly decreases, and her CRP level begins to fall. Which of the following molecular mechanisms is primarily responsible for the rapid anti-inflammatory effects observed following glucocorticoid administration in this patient?

(A) Increased synthesis of IκBα, leading to the inhibition of NF-κB nuclear translocation and reduced transcription of pro-inflammatory cytokines like TNF-α and IL-1β.
(B) Direct inhibition of cyclooxygenase-2 (COX-2) enzyme activity, preventing the synthesis of prostaglandins.
(C) Induction of Annexin A1 (Lipocortin-1) synthesis, which inhibits cytosolic phospholipase A2 (cPLA2) activity, reducing the availability of arachidonic acid for eicosanoid synthesis.
(D) Enhanced degradation of pre-formed inflammatory mediators, such as histamine and bradykinin, by increasing the activity of lysosomal enzymes.
(E) Upregulation of glucocorticoid receptors on the surface of immune cells, leading to increased phagocytosis of immune complexes and apoptotic cells.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 45-year-old female with severe rheumatoid arthritis (RA). RA is a chronic autoimmune inflammatory disease primarily affecting joints.
- **Presentation:** Acute flare with intense joint pain, swelling, stiffness, elevated CRP (120 mg/L), and elevated ESR (95 mm/hr). These are classic signs and symptoms of an RA flare, indicating significant systemic inflammation.
- **Intervention:** Oral dexamethasone (a potent glucocorticoid) is administered.
- **Outcome:** Rapid improvement (within 24 hours) in joint pain and decrease in CRP. This rapid response is characteristic of glucocorticoid action.
- **Question:** Asks for the *primary molecular mechanism* responsible for this *rapid* anti-inflammatory effect.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- Glucocorticoids (like dexamethasone) are potent anti-inflammatory agents.
- They exert their effects by binding to intracellular glucocorticoid receptors (GRs).
- The activated GR complex translocates to the nucleus and acts as a transcription factor.
- One of the key genes upregulated by the GR complex is the gene encoding Annexin A1 (also known as Lipocortin-1).
- Annexin A1 is a protein that directly inhibits cytosolic phospholipase A2 (cPLA2).
- cPLA2 is the enzyme responsible for releasing arachidonic acid from membrane phospholipids.
- Arachidonic acid is the precursor for various inflammatory mediators, including prostaglandins (via COX pathway) and leukotrienes (via LOX pathway).
- By inhibiting cPLA2, Annexin A1 reduces the intracellular pool of arachidonic acid, thereby blocking the synthesis of both prostaglandins and leukotrienes at an early, upstream step.
- This inhibition of eicosanoid synthesis is a major contributor to the rapid anti-inflammatory effects of glucocorticoids, explaining the quick relief of symptoms and reduction in inflammatory markers like CRP observed in the patient.
- Other mechanisms of glucocorticoids (like inhibiting NF-κB, increasing IκBα) are also important but often take longer to manifest fully compared to the Annexin A1/cPLA2 pathway.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Increased synthesis of IκBα, leading to the inhibition of NF-κB nuclear translocation and reduced transcription of pro-inflammatory cytokines like TNF-α and IL-1β. This is a crucial mechanism of glucocorticoids, reducing the production of inflammatory cytokines. However, this effect typically takes several hours to days to become significant and is not considered the *primary* mechanism for the *rapid* (within 24 hours) response seen in this vignette. The Annexin A1 pathway acts much faster.
- (B): Direct inhibition of cyclooxygenase-2 (COX-2) enzyme activity, preventing the synthesis of prostaglandins. Glucocorticoids *do* inhibit COX-2 expression (via NF-κB inhibition and other mechanisms), but they do *not* directly inhibit the enzyme activity itself. NSAIDs directly inhibit COX enzyme activity. Furthermore, this mechanism targets only the prostaglandin pathway, not the leukotriene pathway, and its full effect is also not as rapid as the Annexin A1 pathway.
- (D): Enhanced degradation of pre-formed inflammatory mediators, such as histamine and bradykinin, by increasing the activity of lysosomal enzymes. Glucocorticoids do not primarily work by enhancing the degradation of pre-formed mediators like histamine or bradykinin. Their main action is to inhibit the *synthesis* of new inflammatory mediators.
- (E): Upregulation of glucocorticoid receptors on the surface of immune cells, leading to increased phagocytosis of immune complexes and apoptotic cells. Glucocorticoid receptors are primarily *intracellular*. While glucocorticoids influence immune cell function (e.g., promoting apoptosis of lymphocytes), they do not upregulate surface receptors to enhance phagocytosis as a primary anti-inflammatory mechanism in this context.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Glucocorticoids rapidly reduce inflammation by inducing Annexin A1 (Lipocortin-1), which inhibits cPLA2, blocking arachidonic acid release and subsequent eicosanoid (prostaglandin, leukotriene) synthesis. This is a key mechanism for their fast clinical effects. Differentiate this rapid mechanism from slower effects like cytokine inhibition (NF-κB pathway). Key differentials for anti-inflammatory mechanisms include NSAIDs (direct COX inhibition), biologics targeting specific cytokines (TNF-α inhibitors), and other immunomodulators.

### Question 3: Snake Envenomation & Hemolysis
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the mechanism of action of specific snake venom components, particularly secretory phospholipase A2 (sPLA2), leading to tissue damage and hemolysis. (理解特定蛇毒成分的作用机制，特别是分泌型磷脂酶A2 (sPLA2)，导致组织损伤和溶血。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 29-year-old male hiker is bitten on the lower leg by a snake identified as an Eastern diamondback rattlesnake ( *Crotalus adamanteus*). Within 30 minutes, he develops rapidly progressive, severe swelling, ecchymosis, and pain localized to the bite site. He becomes hypotensive and tachycardic. Laboratory studies reveal acute kidney injury (elevated creatinine and BUN), disseminated intravascular coagulation (DIC) with thrombocytopenia and prolonged PT/PTT, and marked intravascular hemolysis (elevated LDH, decreased haptoglobin, hemoglobinuria). A peripheral blood smear shows schistocytes. The patient is immediately treated with antivenom. Which of the following mechanisms is MOST directly responsible for the rapid intravascular hemolysis observed in this patient?

(A) Direct activation of the complement cascade by venom components leading to membrane attack complex formation on erythrocytes.
(B) Induction of antibody-mediated cytotoxicity against erythrocytes by venom-induced neoantigens.
(C) Hydrolysis of membrane phospholipids by venom secretory phospholipase A2 (sPLA2), generating lysophospholipids that disrupt membrane integrity.
(D) Inhibition of erythrocyte membrane ion pumps (e.g., Na+/K+-ATPase) by venom metalloproteinases, leading to osmotic lysis.
(E) Direct enzymatic degradation of erythrocyte cytoskeletal proteins (e.g., spectrin) by venom proteases, causing membrane instability.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient**: 29yo male hiker.
- **Exposure**: Bite by Eastern diamondback rattlesnake (*Crotalus adamanteus*). This species is known for potent hemotoxic venom.
- **Onset**: Rapid (within 30 minutes).
- **Local Effects**: Severe swelling, ecchymosis, pain at the bite site. Indicates local tissue damage and vascular permeability.
- **Systemic Effects**: Hypotension, tachycardia. Suggests shock, likely due to fluid loss, DIC, and/or direct cardiotoxicity (less common with this species but possible).
- **Lab Findings**:
    - Acute kidney injury (AKI): Common in rattlesnake envenomation due to direct nephrotoxicity, hypotension, and myoglobinuria/hemoglobinuria.
    - DIC: Characterized by thrombocytopenia, prolonged PT/PTT. A hallmark of severe rattlesnake envenomation.
    - Intravascular hemolysis: Elevated LDH, decreased haptoglobin, hemoglobinuria, schistocytes on smear. Indicates red blood cell destruction within the vasculature.
- **Treatment**: Antivenom. Standard treatment for significant venomous snake bites.
- **Question**: Mechanism of *rapid intravascular hemolysis*. The key is the speed and the specific venom type.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- Rattlesnake venom, particularly from *Crotalus* species, contains a complex mixture of enzymes and toxins, including metalloproteinases, serine proteases, hyaluronidases, bradykinin-potentiating peptides, and crucially, secretory phospholipase A2 (sPLA2).
- sPLA2 enzymes are potent mediators of tissue damage and inflammation. They act on the sn-2 position of membrane phospholipids (like phosphatidylcholine).
- The hydrolysis of phospholipids by sPLA2 generates lysophospholipids (e.g., lysophosphatidylcholine) and free fatty acids (e.g., arachidonic acid).
- Lysophospholipids are amphipathic molecules that insert into the lipid bilayer, disrupting its structure and increasing membrane permeability. They can form pores or micelles, leading to membrane destabilization and eventual lysis.
- This mechanism directly affects erythrocytes, endothelial cells, platelets, and other cell types, explaining the intravascular hemolysis, DIC, vascular leakage (edema, ecchymosis), and hypotension observed in the patient.
- The rapid onset of hemolysis is characteristic of this direct membrane-damaging effect of sPLA2.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Direct activation of the complement cascade...**: While some toxins might indirectly activate complement, the primary and most rapid mechanism of hemolysis in rattlesnake envenomation is not direct complement activation leading to MAC formation. Complement activation is typically slower and involves antibody binding (classical pathway) or direct binding to microbial surfaces (alternative pathway). This doesn't fit the rapid, direct membrane lysis picture.
- (B) **Induction of antibody-mediated cytotoxicity...**: This mechanism requires the venom to induce the formation of new antigens on erythrocytes (neoantigens) and subsequent antibody production and binding, leading to complement activation or antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC). This is a much slower process, taking hours to days, and is not consistent with the rapid onset of hemolysis described.
- (D) **Inhibition of erythrocyte membrane ion pumps...**: Inhibition of Na+/K+-ATPase would lead to cellular swelling and eventual osmotic lysis, but this is generally a slower process than direct membrane disruption by sPLA2. Furthermore, while some venom components might affect ion transport, the primary mechanism for rapid hemolysis is the direct enzymatic action on membrane lipids.
- (E) **Direct enzymatic degradation of erythrocyte cytoskeletal proteins...**: Venom proteases can degrade cytoskeletal proteins like spectrin, leading to membrane instability and hemolysis. However, the *most direct* and *rapid* mechanism described for rattlesnake venom, especially causing widespread effects like DIC and vascular damage alongside hemolysis, is the action of sPLA2 on membrane phospholipids. While protease activity contributes, sPLA2 is considered the major driver of the acute hemolytic and inflammatory cascade.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- High-Yield Takeaway: Recognize that rattlesnake venom contains potent sPLA2 enzymes that directly hydrolyze membrane phospholipids, generating lysophospholipids that cause rapid membrane permeabilization, leading to intravascular hemolysis, DIC, and tissue damage. (记住响尾蛇毒液含有强效的sPLA2酶，直接水解膜磷脂，产生溶磷脂，导致快速的膜通透性增加，引起血管内溶血、DIC和组织损伤。)
- Differentials: Other causes of acute hemolysis (e.g., microangiopathic hemolytic anemia like TTP/HUS, autoimmune hemolytic anemia, G6PD deficiency crisis) typically lack the specific history of snakebite and associated local/systemic venom effects (DIC, AKI, severe local tissue necrosis). Other snake venoms (e.g., elapids like cobras) may have different primary mechanisms (e.g., neurotoxicity, myotoxicity).

<a id='question-004'></a>

### Question 4: Dietary Fatty Acids and Eicosanoid Synthesis
- **Difficulty**: Medium
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: The competitive inhibition of arachidonic acid metabolism by omega-3 fatty acids (EPA and DHA) leading to the production of less potent pro-inflammatory eicosanoids (3-series prostaglandins and 5-series leukotrienes). (膳食中 Omega-3 脂肪酸 (EPA 和 DHA) 与花生四烯酸竞争代谢，导致产生毒性较弱的促炎 eicosanoids (3-series 前列腺素和 5-series 白三烯))

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 50-year-old male with a history of hyperlipidemia and mild osteoarthritis begins taking a high-dose marine fish oil supplement (containing 4 grams daily of eicosapentaenoic acid [EPA] and docosahexaenoic acid [DHA]) on the advice of his primary care physician. Six months later, a lipid mediator analysis reveals a significant decrease in the levels of 2-series prostaglandins and 4-series leukotrienes, accompanied by a corresponding increase in the levels of 3-series prostaglandins and 5-series leukotrienes. The patient reports subjective improvement in joint stiffness. Which of the following mechanisms best explains this observed shift in inflammatory lipid mediator profile?

(A) EPA and DHA are directly converted into potent pro-inflammatory cytokines, such as TNF-α and IL-1β, which stimulate the production of 2-series prostaglandins and 4-series leukotrienes.
(B) EPA and DHA inhibit the activity of cyclooxygenase (COX) and lipoxygenase (LOX) enzymes, thereby reducing the overall synthesis of all prostaglandins and leukotrienes.
(C) EPA and DHA compete with arachidonic acid (AA) for the active sites of COX and LOX enzymes, leading to the preferential synthesis of less potent inflammatory mediators derived from EPA and DHA.
(D) EPA and DHA are metabolized into reactive oxygen species (ROS) that directly damage cell membranes, triggering an inflammatory response characterized by increased production of 2-series prostaglandins and 4-series leukotrienes.
(E) EPA and DHA increase the expression of phospholipase A2 (PLA2), leading to increased release of arachidonic acid from membrane phospholipids, which is subsequently metabolized into potent pro-inflammatory 2-series prostaglandins and 4-series leukotrienes.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 50yo male with hyperlipidemia and osteoarthritis, suggesting a potential inflammatory component.
- **Intervention:** High-dose marine fish oil supplement (EPA and DHA). This is the key intervention.
- **Outcome:** Shift in lipid mediator profile: Decrease in 2-series prostaglandins and 4-series leukotrienes (derived from arachidonic acid, AA), increase in 3-series prostaglandins and 5-series leukotrienes (derived from EPA and DHA).
- **Subjective Improvement:** Reduced joint stiffness, consistent with reduced inflammation.
- **Question:** Mechanism explaining the shift in eicosanoid profile.

The core clue is the change in eicosanoid profile after EPA/DHA supplementation. 2-series prostaglandins (PGE2, PGI2) and 4-series leukotrienes (LTB4, LTC4, LTD4, LTE4) are potent mediators of inflammation, pain, fever, and chemotaxis, derived from arachidonic acid (AA). 3-series prostaglandins and 5-series leukotrienes are derived from EPA and DHA, respectively, and are generally considered less potent inflammatory mediators. The question asks for the mechanism linking EPA/DHA intake to this specific shift.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **Arachidonic Acid Cascade:** Arachidonic acid (AA), an omega-6 fatty acid, is released from membrane phospholipids by phospholipase A2 (PLA2).
- **COX Pathway:** AA is metabolized by cyclooxygenase (COX) enzymes (COX-1 and COX-2) into prostaglandin H2 (PGH2). PGH2 is further converted by specific synthases into various prostanoids, including PGE2 (potent vasodilator, pain, fever), PGI2 (prostacyclin, inhibits platelet aggregation, vasodilator), and TXA2 (thromboxane A2, promotes platelet aggregation, vasoconstriction). These are the 2-series prostaglandins.
- **LOX Pathway:** AA is also metabolized by lipoxygenases (LOX) into hydroperoxyeicosatetraenoic acids (HPETEs). These are further converted into leukotrienes (LTB4, LTC4, LTD4, LTE4). LTB4 is a potent chemoattractant for neutrophils, while LTC4, LTD4, and LTE4 (cysteinyl leukotrienes) cause bronchoconstriction, increased vascular permeability, and mucus secretion. These are the 4-series leukotrienes.
- **EPA/DHA Metabolism:** Eicosapentaenoic acid (EPA), an omega-3 fatty acid, can also be released by PLA2 and metabolized by COX and LOX enzymes. COX metabolism of EPA yields PGH3, which is converted to 3-series prostaglandins (e.g., PGE3). LOX metabolism of EPA yields HPETE3, which is converted to 5-series leukotrienes (e.g., LTB5).
- **Competition:** EPA and AA compete for the active sites of both COX and LOX enzymes. DHA can also be metabolized by LOX, yielding 5-series leukotrienes.
- **Potency Difference:** 3-series prostaglandins (e.g., PGE3) and 5-series leukotrienes (e.g., LTB5) are generally less potent inflammatory mediators compared to their 2-series and 4-series counterparts derived from AA. For example, PGE3 has about 1/10th the potency of PGE2 in inducing fever, and LTB5 has about 1/100th the potency of LTB4 in neutrophil chemotaxis.
- **Clinical Effect:** Supplementation with high doses of EPA and DHA leads to increased levels of EPA and DHA available for metabolism. This increased availability, coupled with competition for COX and LOX enzymes, results in a shift away from AA-derived eicosanoids (2-series PGs, 4-series LTs) towards EPA/DHA-derived eicosanoids (3-series PGs, 5-series LTs), leading to a net reduction in overall inflammatory signaling. This aligns with the observed decrease in pro-inflammatory mediators and the patient's subjective improvement.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): **Incorrect.** EPA and DHA are not directly converted into pro-inflammatory cytokines like TNF-α or IL-1β. Cytokines *stimulate* inflammation, including the release of AA and the expression of COX/LOX, but EPA/DHA's primary effect is on the eicosanoid synthesis pathway itself, not cytokine production.
- (B): **Incorrect.** While EPA and DHA *can* inhibit COX and LOX activity to some extent, their primary mechanism for altering the eicosanoid profile is *competition* for the enzyme active sites, not complete inhibition. Complete inhibition would reduce *all* eicosanoids, not just the AA-derived ones, and wouldn't explain the increase in 3-series and 5-series mediators.
- (D): **Incorrect.** EPA and DHA are not primarily metabolized into ROS. While lipid peroxidation can occur, it's not the main mechanism explaining the shift in eicosanoid profile. Furthermore, ROS typically *promote* inflammation, whereas EPA/DHA supplementation leads to reduced inflammation.
- (E): **Incorrect.** EPA and DHA do not increase PLA2 activity. In fact, some studies suggest omega-3 fatty acids might *decrease* PLA2 activity or alter its substrate specificity, potentially reducing AA release. Increased PLA2 activity would lead to *more* AA release, favoring the production of pro-inflammatory 2-series PGs and 4-series LTs, which is the opposite of what is observed.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):

<a id='question-005'></a>

### Question 5: Platelet Activation and Arachidonate Metabolism
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Platelet activation involves phospholipase C (PLC) hydrolysis of PIP2 into IP3 and DAG. Diacylglycerol lipase (DGL) then hydrolyzes DAG into arachidonic acid (AA) and a fatty acid, providing substrate for thromboxane A2 (TXA2) synthesis via cyclooxygenase (COX). This pathway is distinct from the phospholipase A2 (PLA2) pathway involving arachidonyl-phosphatidylcholine.

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 55-year-old male with a history of hypertension and hyperlipidemia presents to the emergency department complaining of acute onset chest pain radiating to his left arm. An electrocardiogram (ECG) shows ST-segment elevation in leads II, III, and aVF, consistent with an inferior myocardial infarction. Initial laboratory tests reveal elevated troponin levels and prolonged platelet aggregation time. To investigate the biochemical mechanisms underlying platelet activation during the thrombotic event, researchers perform an in vitro experiment. Platelets are isolated from the patient's blood and stimulated with ADP. Biochemical analysis reveals increased levels of inositol trisphosphate (IP3) and diacylglycerol (DAG). Subsequent measurement of free arachidonic acid levels shows a significant increase. The researchers then add a specific inhibitor targeting the enzyme responsible for hydrolyzing DAG into arachidonic acid and a free fatty acid. This inhibition leads to a marked decrease in the subsequent synthesis of thromboxane A2 (TXA2) by the activated platelets.

Which of the following enzymes is most likely inhibited by the researchers to decrease thromboxane A2 synthesis in this experiment?

(A) Phospholipase A2 (PLA2)
(B) Phospholipase C (PLC)
(C) Cyclooxygenase (COX)
(D) Diacylglycerol lipase (DGL)
(E) Inositol monophosphatase

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 55-year-old male, hypertension, hyperlipidemia, acute chest pain (MI), ECG changes (inferior MI), elevated troponin, prolonged platelet aggregation time. This establishes a clinical context of acute coronary thrombosis involving platelet activation.
- **Experimental Setup:** Platelets stimulated with ADP (a potent platelet agonist).
- **Biochemical Findings:** Increased IP3 and DAG indicate activation of the phospholipase C (PLC) pathway. PLC cleaves phosphatidylinositol 4,5-bisphosphate (PIP2) into IP3 and DAG.
- **Arachidonic Acid Increase:** Increased free arachidonic acid (AA) confirms that a source of AA is being released.
- **Inhibition Effect:** Addition of a specific inhibitor targeting the enzyme that hydrolyzes DAG leads to decreased TXA2 synthesis. TXA2 is a key product of platelet activation derived from AA via the COX pathway.
- **Question Stem:** Asks to identify the enzyme inhibited that reduces TXA2 synthesis by acting on DAG.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **Platelet Activation:** ADP binding to its receptor (P2Y12) activates heterotrimeric G proteins (Gq).
- **PLC Activation:** Activated Gq stimulates Phospholipase C (PLC).
- **PIP2 Hydrolysis:** PLC hydrolyzes the membrane phospholipid PIP2 into two second messengers: inositol trisphosphate (IP3) and diacylglycerol (DAG).
- **IP3 Action:** IP3 diffuses into the cytoplasm and binds to IP3 receptors on the endoplasmic reticulum, causing the release of intracellular calcium (Ca2+), which further promotes platelet activation (e.g., granule release, cytoskeletal changes).
- **DAG Action:** DAG remains in the plasma membrane. It serves as a substrate for Diacylglycerol Lipase (DGL), also known as DAGL.
- **DGL Action:** DGL hydrolyzes DAG into free arachidonic acid (AA) and a free fatty acid (e.g., palmitic acid).
- **Arachidonic Acid Metabolism:** Free AA is then metabolized by cyclooxygenase (COX-1) in platelets to produce thromboxane A2 (TXA2). TXA2 is a potent vasoconstrictor and platelet aggregator, amplifying the thrombotic response.
- **Inhibition:** The experiment describes inhibiting the enzyme that converts DAG to AA, which subsequently reduces TXA2 production. This enzyme is Diacylglycerol Lipase (DGL).

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) Phospholipase A2 (PLA2): PLA2 hydrolyzes phospholipids, typically releasing AA from arachidonyl-phosphatidylcholine. While PLA2 is involved in inflammation and AA release from other cell types (e.g., leukocytes), the primary pathway for AA release in platelets stimulated by ADP involves PLC and DGL, not PLA2. The vignette specifically points to DAG hydrolysis as the source of AA.
- (B) Phospholipase C (PLC): PLC is upstream of DGL in this pathway. It hydrolyzes PIP2 into IP3 and DAG. Inhibiting PLC would decrease both IP3 and DAG levels, thereby reducing AA release via the DGL pathway and also preventing the Ca2+ release mediated by IP3. However, the vignette states that DAG levels are *increased* upon stimulation, and the inhibitor specifically targets the enzyme acting *on* DAG.
- (C) Cyclooxygenase (COX): COX is the enzyme that converts AA into prostaglandins and thromboxanes (like TXA2). While inhibiting COX would also decrease TXA2 synthesis, the vignette specifies that the inhibitor acts on the enzyme responsible for generating AA *from DAG*. Inhibiting COX would block the *downstream* conversion of AA, not the *upstream* generation of AA from DAG.
- (E) Inositol monophosphatase: This enzyme dephosphorylates inositol phosphates (like IP3) back to inositol. Inhibiting it would prolong the action of IP3 and increase intracellular calcium, potentially enhancing platelet activation, but it would not directly affect the DAG pathway or AA release from DAG.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- **High-Yield Takeaway:** Platelet activation via ADP involves the PLC pathway, generating IP3 and DAG. DAG is hydrolyzed by DGL to release arachidonic acid, which is then converted to TXA2 by COX. Understanding this specific pathway for AA release in platelets is crucial.
- **Differentials:** Remember that AA can be released via PLA2 (e.g., from membrane phospholipids like phosphatidylcholine) in other cell types, but in platelets activated by agonists like ADP, the PLC/DGL pathway is dominant. Also, distinguish between the roles of PLC, DGL, and COX in the arachidonic acid cascade.

<a id='question-006'></a>

### Question 6: Inflammatory Signaling Pathway
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of the intracellular signaling cascade initiated by bacterial lipopolysaccharide (LPS) in macrophages, specifically focusing on the role of calcium influx and the calcium-dependent translocation of cytosolic phospholipase A2 (cPLA2) to the nuclear envelope and endoplasmic reticulum, leading to the production of arachidonic acid, a precursor for eicosanoids. (考察脂多糖(LPS)在巨噬细胞中引发的细胞内信号通路，特别是钙离子内流和依赖钙离子的胞内磷脂酶A2 (cPLA2) 易位到核包膜和内质网的作用，从而产生花生四烯酸，它是类二十碳烯酸的先体。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A researcher is using live-cell imaging to study the inflammatory response of a murine macrophage cell line to bacterial lipopolysaccharide (LPS). The experiment involves stimulating the macrophages with LPS and monitoring the intracellular localization of cytosolic phospholipase A2 (cPLA2) using a fluorescently tagged cPLA2 protein. Following LPS stimulation, the researchers observe a rapid increase in intracellular calcium concentration ([Ca2+]i). Subsequently, they observe that the fluorescently tagged cPLA2 protein rapidly translocates from the cytosol to the nuclear envelope and the endoplasmic reticulum. This translocation is dependent on the presence of calcium ions. The researcher hypothesizes that this calcium-dependent translocation of cPLA2 is crucial for initiating the synthesis of inflammatory mediators. Which of the following molecular mechanisms best explains the observed calcium-dependent translocation of cPLA2 to the nuclear envelope and endoplasmic reticulum following LPS stimulation?

(A) LPS activates Gq protein, leading to activation of phospholipase C (PLC), which hydrolyzes phosphatidylinositol 4,5-bisphosphate (PIP2) into inositol trisphosphate (IP3) and diacylglycerol (DAG). IP3 binds to receptors on the endoplasmic reticulum, causing calcium release into the cytosol, which then binds to the C2 domain of cPLA2, promoting its translocation to the plasma membrane for arachidonic acid release.
(B) LPS activates Gq protein, leading to activation of phospholipase C (PLC), which hydrolyzes phosphatidylinositol 4,5-bisphosphate (PIP2) into inositol trisphosphate (IP3) and diacylglycerol (DAG). IP3 binds to receptors on the endoplasmic reticulum, causing calcium release into the cytosol. This increased cytosolic calcium binds to the C2 domain of cPLA2, inducing a conformational change that facilitates its binding to negatively charged phospholipids on the nuclear envelope and endoplasmic reticulum, thereby mediating its translocation.
(C) LPS activates Gi protein, leading to inhibition of adenylyl cyclase and a decrease in cyclic AMP (cAMP) levels. Decreased cAMP activates protein kinase A (PKA), which phosphorylates cPLA2, promoting its translocation to the plasma membrane for arachidonic acid release.
(D) LPS activates Gs protein, leading to activation of adenylyl cyclase and an increase in cyclic AMP (cAMP) levels. Increased cAMP activates protein kinase A (PKA), which phosphorylates cPLA2, promoting its translocation to the plasma membrane for arachidonic acid release.
(E) LPS directly binds to Toll-like receptor 4 (TLR4) on the macrophage surface, activating downstream kinases like IκB kinase (IKK). IKK phosphorylates IκB, leading to its degradation and the translocation of NF-κB to the nucleus, where it promotes the transcription of genes encoding cPLA2.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **LPS stimulation of macrophages:** LPS is a potent activator of the innate immune system, primarily through Toll-like receptor 4 (TLR4) on macrophages. This sets the stage for an inflammatory response.
- **Live-cell imaging of cPLA2:** The use of live-cell imaging allows observation of dynamic cellular processes in real-time. The focus on cPLA2 indicates the question is about phospholipase activity.
- **Intracellular calcium surge:** LPS stimulation leads to an increase in intracellular calcium concentration ([Ca2+]i). This is a crucial second messenger in many signaling pathways, including inflammation.
- **cPLA2 translocation to nuclear envelope and ER:** The key observation is that cPLA2 moves from the cytosol to specific intracellular membranes (nuclear envelope and ER) after the calcium surge. This is not the typical translocation to the plasma membrane often associated with cPLA2 activation for arachidonic acid release.
- **Calcium-dependent translocation:** The experiment explicitly states that this translocation is dependent on calcium ions. This points towards a calcium-binding protein mediating the process.
- **Question asks for the mechanism:** The question asks for the molecular mechanism explaining this specific calcium-dependent translocation event.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **LPS and TLR4 Activation:** LPS binds to TLR4, initiating intracellular signaling cascades.
- **Gq Protein Activation:** TLR4 signaling often involves the activation of heterotrimeric G proteins, particularly Gq.
- **Phospholipase C (PLC) Activation:** Activated Gq stimulates PLC.
- **PIP2 Hydrolysis:** PLC hydrolyzes the membrane phospholipid PIP2 into two second messengers: IP3 and DAG.
- **Calcium Release:** IP3 diffuses through the cytosol and binds to IP3 receptors (ligand-gated calcium channels) on the endoplasmic reticulum (ER) membrane.
- **[Ca2+]i Increase:** Binding of IP3 opens the calcium channels, causing a rapid release of stored calcium ions from the ER lumen into the cytosol, leading to a significant increase in [Ca2+]i.
- **cPLA2 Structure and Function:** cPLA2 is a cytosolic enzyme that hydrolyzes membrane phospholipids, primarily phosphatidylcholine, to release arachidonic acid. Arachidonic acid is a precursor for various inflammatory mediators like prostaglandins and leukotrienes.
- **Calcium Binding and Translocation:** cPLA2 contains a C2 domain (C2 domain), which is a calcium-binding domain. When cytosolic [Ca2+]i increases, calcium ions bind to the C2 domain of cPLA2.
- **Conformational Change and Membrane Binding:** Calcium binding induces a conformational change in cPLA2, exposing hydrophobic residues. This allows cPLA2 to bind to negatively charged phospholipids (like phosphatidylserine) present on the inner leaflet of the plasma membrane, the nuclear envelope, and the ER membrane.
- **Translocation Mechanism:** This calcium-dependent binding to membrane phospholipids mediates the translocation of cPLA2 from the cytosol to these specific membrane locations. The question specifies translocation to the nuclear envelope and ER, which is distinct from the more commonly described plasma membrane translocation for arachidonic acid release, but still mediated by the same calcium-dependent mechanism involving the C2 domain. The proximity to cyclooxygenase (COX) enzymes, often associated with the nuclear envelope and ER, is relevant for subsequent arachidonic acid metabolism.
- **Therefore:** The sequence is LPS -> TLR4 -> Gq -> PLC -> PIP2 -> IP3 -> ER Ca2+ release -> Increased cytosolic [Ca2+]i -> Ca2+ binds to cPLA2 C2 domain -> Conformational change -> Binding to negatively charged lipids on nuclear envelope/ER -> Translocation.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): This option describes the typical pathway leading to plasma membrane translocation of cPLA2 for arachidonic acid release. While LPS does activate Gq/PLC/IP3/Ca2+ release, the final step described (Ca2+ binding promoting translocation to the *plasma membrane*) is incorrect for the specific scenario described in the vignette (translocation to the *nuclear envelope and ER*). The mechanism of Ca2+ binding to the C2 domain is correct, but the target membrane is wrong.
- (C): This option describes a Gs/cAMP/PKA pathway, which is generally associated with inhibition of inflammation or other cellular processes, not the primary LPS-induced calcium surge and cPLA2 translocation described. LPS primarily activates Gq, not Gi. Furthermore, this pathway involves phosphorylation, not direct calcium-dependent membrane binding via a C2 domain.
- (D): Similar to (C), this option describes a Gs/cAMP/PKA pathway. LPS

<a id='question-007'></a>

### Question 7: Neuroinflammation & Pain Modulation
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: The role of endocannabinoid metabolism (specifically FAAH and MAGL) in regulating neuroinflammation and pain by controlling the availability of arachidonic acid for prostaglandin synthesis via COX-2 in microglia. (内源性大麻素代谢（FAAH和MAGL）在调节神经炎症和疼痛中的作用，通过控制微胶质细胞中COX-2合成前列腺素的 Arachidonic acid 可用性。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A research team is investigating novel therapeutic targets for chronic neuropathic pain associated with neuroinflammation. They hypothesize that modulating the endocannabinoid system could reduce pain by dampening microglial activation and subsequent prostaglandin production. In vitro experiments using primary murine microglia demonstrate that activation with lipopolysaccharide (LPS) induces the expression of cyclooxygenase-2 (COX-2) and increases the production of prostaglandin E2 (PGE2). The researchers then perform experiments where they genetically knock out either fatty acid amide hydrolase (FAAH) or monoacylglycerol lipase (MAGL) in the microglia. They find that knockout of FAAH leads to increased levels of anandamide (AEA), decreased levels of arachidonic acid (AA), and a significant reduction in LPS-induced COX-2 expression and PGE2 production. Conversely, knockout of MAGL leads to increased levels of 2-arachidonoylglycerol (2-AG), decreased levels of arachidonic acid (AA), and a similar significant reduction in LPS-induced COX-2 expression and PGE2 production. However, when the researchers treat the microglia with exogenous arachidonic acid (AA) in both FAAH knockout and MAGL knockout conditions, the reduction in COX-2 expression and PGE2 production is reversed, and levels approach those seen in wild-type microglia stimulated with LPS. Based on these findings, which of the following best explains the mechanism by which FAAH and MAGL contribute to neuroinflammation and pain modulation in this model?

(A) FAAH and MAGL directly inhibit COX-2 activity, thereby reducing prostaglandin synthesis.
(B) FAAH and MAGL hydrolyze endocannabinoids, releasing arachidonic acid which is then metabolized by COX-2 to produce pro-inflammatory prostaglandins, amplifying the inflammatory response.
(C) FAAH and MAGL promote the synthesis of endocannabinoids, which directly activate cannabinoid receptors on microglia, leading to anti-inflammatory effects.
(D) FAAH and MAGL degrade arachidonic acid, preventing its conversion to pro-inflammatory prostaglandins by COX-2.
(E) FAAH and MAGL inhibit the release of LPS, thereby reducing microglial activation and subsequent prostaglandin production.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Setting:** Research study on neuropathic pain and neuroinflammation.
- **Hypothesis:** Modulating endocannabinoid system reduces pain by dampening microglial activation and prostaglandin production.
- **Experiment:** LPS activates microglia, inducing COX-2 and PGE2.
- **FAAH Knockout:** Increased AEA, decreased AA, reduced COX-2/PGE2.
- **MAGL Knockout:** Increased 2-AG, decreased AA, reduced COX-2/PGE2.
- **AA Rescue:** Exogenous AA reverses the reduction in COX-2/PGE2 in both knockouts.
- **Question:** Mechanism of FAAH/MAGL action in neuroinflammation/pain.

**Key Clues:**
- FAAH breaks down AEA (an amide).
- MAGL breaks down 2-AG (a glycerol).
- Both AEA and 2-AG are endocannabinoids.
- Both FAAH and MAGL release AA upon hydrolysis of their respective substrates.
- Knockout of FAAH or MAGL leads to *less* AA available.
- Less AA leads to *less* COX-2/PGE2 production (anti-inflammatory effect).
- Adding back AA reverses this effect, indicating AA is the crucial intermediate.
- The overall effect of FAAH/MAGL activity (hydrolyzing endocannabinoids) is to *increase* the pool of AA available for COX-2, thus *promoting* inflammation.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **Endocannabinoid System:** The endocannabinoid system involves endogenous ligands (AEA, 2-AG), receptors (CB1, CB2), and metabolizing enzymes.
- **AEA Metabolism:** Anandamide (AEA) is an N-arachidonoylethanolamine. It is synthesized "on demand" from membrane phospholipids. FAAH (Fatty Acid Amide Hydrolase) is the primary enzyme responsible for hydrolyzing AEA into arachidonic acid (AA) and ethanolamine.
- **2-AG Metabolism:** 2-Arachidonoylglycerol (2-AG) is a lysophosphatidate. It is also synthesized "on demand". MAGL (Monoacylglycerol Lipase) is the primary enzyme responsible for hydrolyzing 2-AG into AA and lysophosphatidic acid.
- **Role in Neuroinflammation:** Microglia are the resident immune cells of the central nervous system. Activation by stimuli like LPS triggers an inflammatory cascade. A key step is the upregulation of COX-2, which converts AA into pro-inflammatory prostaglandins like PGE2. PGE2 contributes to pain sensitization and further inflammation.
- **FAAH/MAGL and AA:** Both FAAH and MAGL, by hydrolyzing their respective endocannabinoid substrates (AEA and 2-AG), release AA. This released AA serves as a substrate pool for COX-2.
- **FAAH/MAGL Knockout:** When FAAH or MAGL is knocked out, the breakdown of AEA or 2-AG is impaired. This leads to an accumulation of the respective endocannabinoid. More importantly for this question, it leads to a *decrease* in the release of AA from these endocannabinoids.
- **Reduced AA -> Reduced Prostaglandins:** With less AA available, COX-2 activity is reduced, leading to lower levels of PGE2 production. This explains the observed anti-inflammatory effect in the FAAH and MAGL knockout microglia.
- **AA Rescue Experiment:** The fact that adding exogenous AA reverses the anti-inflammatory effect confirms that the reduced AA availability is the critical factor mediating the effect of FAAH/MAGL knockout.
- **Conclusion:** Therefore, the normal function of FAAH and MAGL in this context is to hydrolyze endocannabinoids, thereby releasing AA, which fuels COX-2-mediated prostaglandin synthesis, contributing to the inflammatory response and potentially pain modulation.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Incorrect.** FAAH and MAGL do not directly inhibit COX-2. They provide the substrate (AA) *for* COX-2. The experiment shows that *lack* of FAAH/MAGL activity (knockout) leads to *reduced* COX-2 activity, not direct inhibition.
- (C) **Incorrect.** While endocannabinoids (AEA, 2-AG) can activate cannabinoid receptors (CB1/CB2) on microglia and exert anti-inflammatory effects, the question focuses on the *metabolic* role of FAAH and MAGL. The experiment shows that the *knockout* of these enzymes (leading to *increased* endocannabinoid levels) reduces inflammation, but this is mediated by the *decrease* in AA availability, not direct receptor activation by the accumulated endocannabinoids (though that might also contribute, it's not the primary mechanism highlighted by the AA rescue experiment). The core finding is the link between FAAH/MAGL, AA release, and COX-2/PGE2.
- (D) **Incorrect.** FAAH and MAGL do not degrade AA. They degrade endocannabinoids (AEA and 2-AG), *releasing* AA in the process. Enzymes like lipoxygenases (LOX) or cytochrome P450 epoxygenases metabolize AA.
- (E) **Incorrect.** FAAH and MAGL do not directly affect LPS release or microglial activation by LPS. LPS binds to TLR4 receptors on microglia, initiating intracellular signaling cascades that lead to gene transcription (like COX-2) and protein synthesis (like PGE2).

<a id='question-008'></a>

### Question 8: Acute Respiratory Distress Syndrome (ARDS) Pathogenesis
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: The question tests the understanding of the coordinated synthesis of Platelet-Activating Factor (PAF) and arachidonic acid metabolites (eicosanoids) from membrane phospholipids during acute inflammation, specifically in the context of ARDS pathogenesis. (考察急性炎症期间细胞膜磷脂酰胆碱被切割后，花生四烯酸及其衍生物和PAF协调合成的机制，特别是在ARDS发病机制中的作用。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old male with a history of chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD) and recent community-acquired pneumonia is admitted to the intensive care unit (ICU) with worsening respiratory distress. He is febrile (38.5°C), tachycardic (115 bpm), and tachypneic (30 breaths/min) with an oxygen saturation of 88% on room air. Arterial blood gas analysis reveals severe hypoxemia (PaO2 55 mmHg) and respiratory acidosis (pH 7.30, PaCO2 55 mmHg). Chest X-ray shows bilateral diffuse alveolar infiltrates consistent with Acute Respiratory Distress Syndrome (ARDS). Bronchoalveolar lavage (BAL) fluid analysis reveals a significant increase in neutrophils and inflammatory mediators. Subsequent research focuses on the specific lipid mediators involved in the endothelial and epithelial injury characteristic of ARDS. Which of the following mechanisms BEST describes the coordinated generation of key inflammatory mediators contributing to the pathogenesis of ARDS in this patient?

(A) Phospholipase A2 (PLA2) cleaves phosphatidylserine, releasing lysophosphatidylcholine and arachidonic acid, which is then metabolized by cyclooxygenase (COX) to form prostaglandins.
(B) Cytosolic phospholipase A2 (cPLA2) cleaves 1-O-alkyl-2-arachidonoyl-sn-glycero-3-phosphocholine, releasing arachidonic acid and lyso-PAF, which is subsequently acetylated by lyso-PAF acetyltransferase to form PAF.
(C) Lipoxygenase (LOX) directly metabolizes phosphatidylcholine to form leukotrienes, while simultaneously cleaving PAF from the same phospholipid molecule.
(D) Secretory phospholipase A2 (sPLA2) cleaves sphingomyelin, releasing ceramide and arachidonic acid, which then activates thromboxane synthase to produce PAF.
(E) Diacylglycerol lipase (DGL) hydrolyzes phosphatidylcholine, releasing phosphatidic acid and PAF, while arachidonic acid is released via an unrelated pathway involving phospholipase C (PLC).

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 62-year-old male with COPD, pneumonia, presenting with severe respiratory distress, hypoxemia, respiratory acidosis, and bilateral alveolar infiltrates – classic signs and symptoms of ARDS.
- **BAL Findings:** Increased neutrophils and inflammatory mediators confirm an acute inflammatory process in the lungs.
- **Question Focus:** The question asks for the mechanism of coordinated generation of key inflammatory mediators (specifically mentioning PAF and implying arachidonic acid metabolites) during ARDS pathogenesis.
- **Key Clue:** The coordinated generation implies that both mediators arise from the same precursor molecule or pathway.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **ARDS Pathogenesis:** ARDS involves diffuse alveolar damage, increased capillary permeability, and inflammation. Lipid mediators play a crucial role.
- **Phospholipids as Precursors:** Membrane phospholipids, particularly phosphatidylcholine (PC), are major sources of arachidonic acid and PAF during inflammation.
- **cPLA2 Activation:** Cytosolic phospholipase A2 (cPLA2) is activated by inflammatory stimuli (e.g., cytokines, bacterial products, hypoxia).
- **Specific Substrate:** cPLA2 preferentially cleaves a specific type of phosphatidylcholine, 1-O-alkyl-2-arachidonoyl-sn-glycero-3-phosphocholine.
- **Products of cPLA2 Cleavage:** Cleavage of this specific PC substrate by cPLA2 yields two key products:
    - **Arachidonic Acid (AA):** Released from the sn-2 position. AA is the precursor for eicosanoids like prostaglandins (via COX) and leukotrienes (via LOX), which are potent inflammatory mediators.
    - **Lyso-PAF:** The remaining molecule after AA release is lyso-PAF (1-O-alkyl-sn-glycero-3-phosphocholine).
- **PAF Synthesis:** Lyso-PAF is not yet active PAF. It needs to be acetylated at the sn-1 hydroxyl group.
- **Lyso-PAF Acetyltransferase:** This enzyme acetylates lyso-PAF using acetyl-CoA as the acetyl donor.
- **Final Product:** The resulting molecule is Platelet-Activating Factor (PAF), a potent phospholipid mediator involved in inflammation, platelet aggregation, bronchoconstriction, and increased vascular permeability – all relevant to ARDS pathogenesis.
- **Coordination:** This pathway explains the *coordinated* generation: the activation of cPLA2 on a specific phospholipid substrate simultaneously releases the precursor for eicosanoids (AA) and the precursor for PAF (lyso-PAF). Subsequent acetylation completes PAF synthesis. This mechanism is a well-established pathway for PAF generation during inflammation.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- **(A):** Incorrect. Phosphatidylserine is cleaved by PLA2, but this primarily releases lysophosphatidylserine and arachidonic acid. While AA is relevant, this option doesn't describe the specific pathway for PAF generation from phosphatidylcholine, nor does it mention the coordinated release with AA from the specific substrate cleaved by cPLA2.
- **(B):** Correct. This option accurately describes the cPLA2-mediated cleavage of 1-O-alkyl-2-arachidonoyl-sn-glycero-3-phosphocholine, yielding both arachidonic acid (precursor for eicosanoids) and lyso-PAF, which is then converted to PAF. This pathway explains the coordinated synthesis.
- **(C):** Incorrect. Lipoxygenase (LOX) metabolizes arachidonic acid, not phosphatidylcholine, to form leukotrienes. PAF is synthesized from lyso-PAF, not directly cleaved from phosphatidylcholine by LOX. This option incorrectly links LOX to both leukotriene and PAF synthesis from PC.
- **(D):** Incorrect. Secretory phospholipase A2 (sPLA2) primarily cleaves phospholipids with external amine groups (like sphingomyelin) or phospholipids on the outer leaflet, releasing lysophospholipids and fatty acids (like ceramide and arachidonic acid). While sPLA2 can release AA, it is not the primary enzyme responsible for the specific cleavage of 1-O-alkyl-2-arachidonoyl-sn-glycero-3-phosphocholine that yields both AA and lyso-PAF. Thromboxane synthase acts on arachidonic acid, not PAF.
- **(E):** Incorrect. Diacylglycerol lipase (DGL) cleaves diacylglycerol (DAG), not phosphatidylcholine. PAF is not a product of DGL activity. Arachidonic acid release via PLC involves the cleavage of phosphatidylinositol bisphosphate (PIP2) into IP3 and DAG, followed by DAG cleavage by DGL, but this pathway does not directly produce PAF or involve the specific substrate cleaved by cPLA2 described in option B.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- **High-Yield Takeaway:** Understand the coordinated synthesis of arachidonic acid (for eicosanoids) and PAF from the specific phospholipid substrate 1-O-alkyl-2-arachidonoyl-sn-glycero-3-phosphocholine via cPLA2 activation during inflammation. Recognize PAF as a key mediator in ARDS pathogenesis.
- **Differentials:** Differentiate the roles and substrates of different phospholipases (cPLA2, sPLA2, PLA2G) and enzymes involved in eicosanoid (COX, LOX) and PAF synthesis. Understand the precursors for different lipid mediators (e.g., AA from phospholipids, PAF

<a id='question-009'></a>

### Question 9: Gastric Ulceration & NSAID Use
- **Difficulty**: Medium
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: The differential roles of cyclooxygenase (COX) isoforms, specifically COX-1 (constitutive, housekeeping functions like gastric protection) and COX-2 (inducible, inflammation), and the consequences of inhibiting each.

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old male with a history of osteoarthritis presents to the emergency department complaining of severe epigastric pain and melena. He reports taking naproxen, a non-selective NSAID, daily for the past 3 months for his joint pain. On examination, he is tachycardic (HR 110 bpm) and hypotensive (BP 90/60 mmHg). Laboratory results show hemoglobin of 7.5 g/dL and hematocrit of 22%. Upper endoscopy reveals multiple large, bleeding ulcers in the gastric antrum and duodenum. A biopsy of the gastric mucosa shows disruption of the epithelial lining and submucosal hemorrhage. Which of the following mechanisms BEST explains the development of these gastric ulcers in this patient?

(A) Inhibition of COX-2 by naproxen leads to decreased production of prostaglandins that protect the gastric mucosa, while COX-1 inhibition has minimal effect.
(B) Inhibition of COX-1 by naproxen leads to decreased production of prostaglandins that protect the gastric mucosa, while COX-2 inhibition has minimal effect.
(C) Inhibition of both COX-1 and COX-2 by naproxen leads to decreased production of prostaglandins that promote gastric acid secretion, increasing ulcer risk.
(D) Inhibition of both COX-1 and COX-2 by naproxen leads to decreased production of prostaglandins that inhibit gastric acid secretion, leading to hypergastrinemia and ulcer formation.
(E) Inhibition of COX-2 by naproxen leads to decreased production of thromboxane A2 in platelets, increasing bleeding risk, while COX-1 inhibition has minimal effect on gastric mucosa.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 62-year-old male with osteoarthritis. This suggests chronic pain management, potentially involving NSAIDs.
- **Medication:** Naproxen, a non-selective NSAID. This is a crucial clue, indicating inhibition of both COX-1 and COX-2.
- **Symptoms:** Severe epigastric pain and melena (black, tarry stools indicating upper GI bleeding). These point towards a peptic ulcer, likely bleeding.
- **Vitals/Labs:** Tachycardia, hypotension, low hemoglobin/hematocrit confirm significant blood loss (hemorrhagic shock).
- **Endoscopy:** Multiple bleeding ulcers in the gastric antrum and duodenum. Confirms the diagnosis of peptic ulcer disease.
- **Biopsy:** Disruption of epithelial lining and submucosal hemorrhage. Consistent with ulceration and bleeding.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the mechanism explaining the *gastric ulcers* specifically.

The core issue is the link between NSAID use (naproxen) and gastric ulceration. NSAIDs inhibit COX enzymes. The question hinges on understanding which COX isoform is primarily responsible for maintaining gastric mucosal integrity.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **Prostaglandins and Gastric Mucosa:** Prostaglandins (PGs), particularly PGE2 and PGI2, play a critical role in protecting the gastric mucosa. They achieve this through several mechanisms:
    - Stimulating mucus and bicarbonate secretion (forming a protective barrier).
    - Inhibiting gastric acid secretion by parietal cells.
    - Maintaining mucosal blood flow (essential for tissue health and repair).
- **COX Isoforms:**
    - **COX-1 (Constitutive):** Expressed in most tissues, including platelets, gastric mucosa, kidney, and endothelium. It is responsible for "housekeeping" functions, including the continuous production of PGs needed for gastric protection, platelet aggregation (via TXA2), and renal blood flow regulation.
    - **COX-2 (Inducible):** Normally expressed at low levels in some tissues but is rapidly induced (50-fold or more) by inflammatory stimuli (e.g., cytokines like TNF-α, IL-1β; bacterial LPS) in cells like endothelial cells, macrophages, and synovial fibroblasts. It is the primary source of PGs involved in inflammation, pain, and fever.
- **NSAID Mechanism:** Non-selective NSAIDs like naproxen inhibit both COX-1 and COX-2. Selective COX-2 inhibitors (coxibs) primarily target COX-2.
- **Gastric Ulcer Pathogenesis:** Inhibition of COX-1 reduces the constitutive production of protective PGs in the gastric mucosa. This leads to:
    - Decreased mucus and bicarbonate secretion.
    - Increased susceptibility to acid damage.
    - Reduced mucosal blood flow.
    - Impaired epithelial cell repair.
    - The net effect is a breakdown of the mucosal barrier, leading to inflammation, erosion, and ulceration.
- **COX-2 Role in Gastric Ulcers:** While COX-2 is induced during inflammation, its constitutive role in gastric protection is considered minor compared to COX-1. Some studies suggest COX-2 might even have pro-ulcerogenic effects by increasing acid secretion or reducing bicarbonate secretion in certain contexts, but the primary mechanism of NSAID-induced ulcers is COX-1 inhibition.
- **Therefore:** Naproxen inhibits COX-1, reducing protective PGs in the gastric mucosa, leading to ulceration. Inhibition of COX-2 has a much smaller direct effect on gastric mucosal protection under normal conditions, although it contributes to the overall anti-inflammatory effect and potential side effects in other tissues.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): **Incorrect.** This reverses the roles of COX-1 and COX-2 in gastric protection. COX-1 is the primary isoform responsible for constitutive PG production protecting the stomach. Inhibiting COX-2 has minimal direct impact on this baseline protection.
- (C): **Incorrect.** While NSAIDs do decrease prostaglandin production, the primary effect relevant to ulceration is the *reduction* of protective PGs, not the promotion of acid secretion. Reduced PGs lead to decreased inhibition of acid secretion (i.e., more acid), but the main mechanism is the loss of the protective barrier itself. Also, this option incorrectly implies both COX-1 and COX-2 inhibition *promotes* acid secretion, which is not the direct mechanism of ulceration.
- (D): **Incorrect.** Similar to (C), this option incorrectly states that inhibiting PGs leads to *increased* acid secretion. Inhibition of PGs actually *reduces* the inhibition of acid secretion, leading to a relative increase, but the primary damage is due to the loss of the protective barrier (mucus, bicarbonate, blood flow) mediated by COX-1 derived PGs. Hypergastrinemia is not the primary mechanism of NSAID-induced ulcers.
- (E): **Incorrect.** This option focuses on the effect of COX inhibition on platelets (TXA2 production via COX-1) and bleeding risk, which is true, but it incorrectly states that COX-1 inhibition has minimal effect on the gastric mucosa. The effect on the gastric mucosa is the *primary* reason for the ulcers in this clinical scenario. Also, it incorrectly attributes TXA2 production to COX-2 inhibition.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- **High-Yield Takeaway:** Non-selective NSAIDs inhibit both COX-1 and COX-2. COX-1 inhibition is the primary cause of NSAID-induced gastric ulcers due to the loss of constitutive prostaglandin production essential for gastric mucosal protection. COX-2 is primarily involved in inflammation and pain.
- **Differentials:** Other causes of peptic ulcers include *Helicobacter pylori* infection (most common), Zollinger-Ellison syndrome (gastrinoma leading to hyperacidity), stress ulcers (critical illness), and certain medications (e.g., corticosteroids, bisphosphonates). However, the vignette strongly points to NSAID use as the cause.

<a id='question-010'></a>

### Question 10: Vascular Catheterization Complication
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the role of prostacyclin (PGI2) synthesized by healthy endothelial cells in maintaining vascular tone and inhibiting platelet aggregation, and how its disruption can lead to thrombosis. (理解内皮细胞合成的前列环素 (PGI2) 在维持血管张力和抑制血小板聚集中的作用，以及其破坏如何导致血栓形成。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 60-year-old female with a history of hypertension and hyperlipidemia is undergoing elective percutaneous coronary intervention (PCI) with stent placement via femoral artery access. During the procedure, a vascular catheter is advanced through her femoral artery. Following stent deployment and catheter removal, the patient develops acute onset of severe pain, swelling, and discoloration (cyanosis) of her right lower extremity. Doppler ultrasound reveals complete occlusion of the right femoral artery at the site of catheter insertion. Laboratory tests show an elevated D-dimer level and prolonged prothrombin time (PT) and activated partial thromboplastin time (aPTT). A biopsy of the affected artery wall shows extensive fibrin deposition and platelet aggregates within the lumen, with evidence of endothelial denudation at the site of catheter trauma.

Which of the following mechanisms is MOST likely responsible for the acute thrombosis observed in this patient?

(A) Decreased synthesis of thromboxane A2 (TXA2) by platelets, leading to reduced platelet activation.
(B) Increased synthesis of von Willebrand factor (vWF) by damaged endothelial cells, promoting platelet adhesion.
(C) Decreased synthesis of prostacyclin (PGI2) by damaged endothelial cells, leading to unopposed vasoconstriction and platelet aggregation.
(D) Increased synthesis of nitric oxide (NO) by damaged endothelial cells, leading to excessive vasodilation and endothelial dysfunction.
(E) Decreased synthesis of tissue factor (TF) by damaged endothelial cells, leading to impaired activation of the coagulation cascade.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 60yo female, HTN, hyperlipidemia undergoing PCI via femoral artery access. This sets the stage for potential vascular injury and thrombotic complications.
- **Procedure:** Vascular catheterization (PCI) involves inserting a catheter into an artery, which can damage the endothelium.
- **Clinical Presentation:** Acute onset of severe pain, swelling, cyanosis of the right lower extremity post-procedure. These are classic signs of acute limb ischemia due to arterial occlusion.
- **Imaging:** Doppler ultrasound confirms complete occlusion of the right femoral artery at the catheter insertion site.
- **Labs:** Elevated D-dimer (indicates fibrinolysis/thrombosis), prolonged PT/aPTT (suggests consumption of clotting factors or potentially heparinization, but the primary event is thrombosis).
- **Biopsy:** Extensive fibrin deposition and platelet aggregates within the lumen, endothelial denudation at the site of catheter trauma. This confirms acute thrombosis secondary to endothelial injury.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the MOST likely mechanism responsible for the acute thrombosis.

The key event is endothelial damage caused by the catheter. Healthy endothelium produces vasodilators (like NO and PGI2) and anti-platelet agents (like PGI2). Damage removes this protective layer. The question asks about the *loss* of a protective factor.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **Endothelial Cell Function:** Intact endothelial cells synthesize several substances that regulate vascular tone and hemostasis. Key among these are prostacyclin (PGI2) and nitric oxide (NO), which cause vasodilation and inhibit platelet aggregation. They also produce thrombomodulin, which activates protein C, and tissue factor pathway inhibitor (TFPI), which inhibits coagulation.
- **Endothelial Injury:** Catheterization causes mechanical trauma, leading to endothelial denudation (removal of the endothelial cell layer).
- **Loss of PGI2:** Damaged endothelial cells lose their ability to synthesize PGI2 effectively.
- **PGI2 Function:** PGI2 is a potent vasodilator (acting via IP receptors on vascular smooth muscle cells, stimulating adenylyl cyclase and increasing cAMP) and a powerful inhibitor of platelet aggregation (acting via IP receptors on platelets, stimulating adenylyl cyclase and increasing cAMP, which inhibits platelet activation and aggregation).
- **Consequences of PGI2 Loss:** With reduced PGI2 production, two major effects occur:
    1.  **Vasoconstriction:** The vasodilatory effect of PGI2 is lost, allowing vasoconstrictive signals (like TXA2 from platelets, endothelin) to dominate, contributing to vascular narrowing or occlusion.
    2.  **Platelet Aggregation:** The inhibitory effect of PGI2 on platelets is lost. Platelets become more easily activated and aggregate at the site of injury, especially in the presence of exposed subendothelial collagen and von Willebrand factor (vWF).
- **Thrombus Formation:** The combination of vasoconstriction and increased platelet aggregation leads to the formation of a thrombus (blood clot) within the damaged artery, causing the observed acute limb ischemia.

Therefore, the decreased synthesis of PGI2 by damaged endothelial cells is a critical mechanism contributing to the acute thrombosis.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Decreased synthesis of thromboxane A2 (TXA2) by platelets, leading to reduced platelet activation.** This is incorrect. TXA2 is synthesized by *platelets* and promotes vasoconstriction and platelet aggregation. Decreased TXA2 would *inhibit* thrombosis, not cause it. Aspirin, for example, inhibits TXA2 synthesis and is used to prevent thrombosis.
- (B) **Increased synthesis of von Willebrand factor (vWF) by damaged endothelial cells, promoting platelet adhesion.** This is incorrect. While damaged endothelial cells *do* release vWF from Weibel-Palade bodies, and vWF *is* crucial for platelet adhesion to exposed collagen, the primary defect in this scenario is the loss of the *inhibitory* effect of PGI2. Increased vWF contributes to thrombosis, but the loss of PGI2 is a more direct and potent factor in this specific context of endothelial denudation leading to both vasoconstriction and platelet aggregation. Also, endothelial cells primarily *store* and *release* vWF, they don't typically *synthesize* large amounts de novo in response to injury.
- (D) **Increased synthesis of nitric oxide (NO) by damaged endothelial cells, leading to excessive vasodilation and endothelial dysfunction.** This is incorrect. Similar to PGI2, NO is a vasodilator synthesized by healthy endothelial cells. Endothelial damage *decreases* NO synthesis, contributing to vasoconstriction. Increased NO would cause vasodilation, which would oppose thrombosis.
- (E) **Decreased synthesis of tissue factor (TF) by damaged endothelial cells, leading to impaired activation of the coagulation cascade.** This is incorrect. Healthy endothelial cells express very little TF. Endothelial injury *exposes* TF from subendothelial cells or *increases* TF expression by endothelial cells themselves, which *promotes* coagulation cascade activation and thrombus formation. Decreased TF would inhibit coagulation.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- **High-Yield Takeaway:** Endothelial injury disrupts the balance between pro-thrombotic and anti-thrombotic factors. Loss of endothelial-derived vasodilators (PGI2, NO) and anti-platelet agents (PGI2) promotes vasoconstriction and platelet aggregation, leading to thrombosis. (内皮损伤破坏了促凝和抗凝平衡。失去内皮细胞衍生的血管扩张剂 (PGI2, NO) 和抗血小板因子 (PGI2) 会促进血管收缩和血小板聚集，导致血栓形成。)
- **Differentials:** Consider other causes of acute limb ischemia (e.g., pre-existing atherosclerosis, emboli), but the clinical context (post-catheterization, endothelial denudation) strongly points to thrombosis secondary to endothelial injury. Understand the roles of other endothelial factors like vWF, TF, thrombomodulin, and TFPI in hemostasis and thrombosis. Remember that PGI2 and NO are key vasodilators and anti-platelet agents produced by healthy endothelium.
