<a id='question-032'></a>

### Question 32: Leukotriene Pathway Divergence
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: The conversion of Leukotriene A4 (LTA4) to Leukotriene B4 (LTB4) by Leukotriene A4 hydrolase (LTA4H) occurs specifically in neutrophils and other myeloid cells, leading to the production of a potent chemoattractant, while the conversion of LTA4 to Leukotriene C4 (LTC4) occurs in mast cells and other cells expressing LTC4 synthase, leading to cysteinyl leukotrienes involved in bronchoconstriction and increased vascular permeability. (LTA4的转化：LTA4水解酶 (LTA4H) 将LTA4转化为LTB4，发生在嗜中性粒细胞等髓系细胞中，产生强效趋化因子；LTC4合成酶将LTA4转化为LTC4，发生在肥大细胞等细胞中，产生囊性白三烯，参与支气管收缩和血管通透性增加。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old male with a history of severe, poorly controlled asthma presents to the emergency department with an acute exacerbation characterized by wheezing, shortness of breath, and chest tightness. He reports recent exposure to moldy hay while working on his farm. Physical examination reveals diffuse expiratory wheezing and tachypnea (respiratory rate 30/min). Arterial blood gas analysis shows hypoxemia (PaO2 65 mmHg) and respiratory acidosis (PaCO2 55 mmHg). Sputum analysis reveals an abundance of eosinophils and neutrophils. The patient is treated with high-dose inhaled corticosteroids, bronchodilators, and systemic corticosteroids. Subsequent analysis of bronchoalveolar lavage fluid from this patient demonstrates elevated levels of a lipid mediator known to cause bronchoconstriction, increased vascular permeability, and mucus secretion. This mediator is synthesized via the 5-lipoxygenase pathway and is a product of the metabolism of Leukotriene A4 (LTA4). The enzyme responsible for converting LTA4 into this specific mediator is primarily expressed in mast cells and basophils.

Which of the following enzymes is responsible for the conversion of LTA4 into the mediator found elevated in the bronchoalveolar lavage fluid of this patient?

(A) 5-Lipoxygenase (5-LOX)
(B) Leukotriene A4 Hydrolase (LTA4H)
(C) LTC4 Synthase
(D) Epoxide Hydrolase
(E) Cyclooxygenase-2 (COX-2)

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 62-year-old male with severe asthma exacerbation triggered by mold exposure. Symptoms include wheezing, dyspnea, chest tightness.
- **Physical Exam:** Diffuse wheezing, tachypnea.
- **ABG:** Hypoxemia, respiratory acidosis (consistent with severe asthma exacerbation).
- **Sputum:** Eosinophils and neutrophils (characteristic inflammatory cells in asthma and allergic reactions).
- **Treatment:** Standard asthma exacerbation therapy.
- **Key Finding:** Elevated lipid mediator in bronchoalveolar lavage (BAL) fluid.
- **Mediator Properties:** Causes bronchoconstriction, increased vascular permeability, mucus secretion (hallmarks of cysteinyl leukotrienes).
- **Synthesis Pathway:** Synthesized via the 5-lipoxygenase pathway from arachidonic acid.
- **Precursor:** Product of LTA4 metabolism.
- **Enzyme Location:** The enzyme responsible is primarily expressed in mast cells and basophils.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the specific enzyme converting LTA4 to this mediator in mast cells/basophils.

The vignette describes a classic asthma exacerbation. The key is identifying the mediator responsible for the symptoms (bronchoconstriction, vascular permeability, mucus) and its synthesis pathway. The mediator is derived from LTA4 via the 5-lipoxygenase pathway and is produced primarily by mast cells/basophils. This points directly to the cysteinyl leukotrienes (LTC4, LTD4, LTE4). The enzyme responsible for the first step in their synthesis, converting LTA4 to LTC4, is LTC4 synthase.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
1.  **Initiating Stimulus:** Exposure to mold triggers an inflammatory response in the airways of a susceptible individual (asthmatic).
2.  **Arachidonic Acid Release:** Mast cells and eosinophils degranulate, releasing inflammatory mediators, including arachidonic acid, from membrane phospholipids via phospholipase A2 (PLA2).
3.  **5-Lipoxygenase Pathway:** Arachidonic acid is metabolized by 5-lipoxygenase (5-LOX) to form 5-hydroperoxyeicosatetraenoic acid (5-HPETE).
4.  **LTA4 Formation:** 5-HPETE is converted to the unstable intermediate Leukotriene A4 (LTA4).
5.  **Pathway Divergence:** LTA4 is a crucial branch point. Its fate depends on the cell type and the enzymes expressed.
    *   **In Mast Cells/Basophils:** LTA4 is converted to Leukotriene C4 (LTC4) by the enzyme **LTC4 synthase**. LTC4 is then converted to LTD4 and LTE4. These cysteinyl leukotrienes (LTC4, LTD4, LTE4) are potent mediators of bronchoconstriction, increased vascular permeability (leading to edema), and mucus secretion. They act on CysLT1 receptors on airway smooth muscle, endothelial cells, and goblet cells.
    *   **In Neutrophils/Macrophages:** LTA4 is converted to Leukotriene B4 (LTB4) by the enzyme **Leukotriene A4 hydrolase (LTA4H)**, also known as epoxide hydrolase in this context. LTB4 is a potent chemoattractant for neutrophils and other leukocytes, playing a key role in inflammation.
6.  **Clinical Correlation:** The patient's symptoms (wheezing, bronchoconstriction) and the elevated mediator in BAL fluid (causing bronchoconstriction, vascular permeability, mucus secretion) are consistent with the actions of cysteinyl leukotrienes (LTC4, LTD4, LTE4).
7.  **Enzyme Identification:** Since the mediator is produced in mast cells/basophils (as stated in the vignette) and causes the described effects, it must be LTC4/LTD4/LTE4. The enzyme responsible for converting LTA4 to LTC4 in these cells is LTC4 synthase.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **5-Lipoxygenase (5-LOX):** This enzyme catalyzes the *initial* step in the pathway, converting arachidonic acid to 5-HPETE, which is then converted to LTA4. It does not convert LTA4 to the specific mediator described (LTC4/LTD4/LTE4). While essential for leukotriene synthesis, it's not the enzyme responsible for the LTA4 -> LTC4 conversion.
- (B) **Leukotriene A4 Hydrolase (LTA4H):** This enzyme converts LTA4 to LTB4. LTB4 is primarily produced by neutrophils and macrophages and acts as a chemoattractant. While neutrophils are present in the sputum, the primary mediator causing the *asthma symptoms* described (bronchoconstriction, vascular permeability) is the cysteinyl leukotriene pathway product, and LTB4 is not the main driver of these specific effects in asthma. Furthermore, the vignette specifies the enzyme is primarily expressed in mast cells/basophils, which produce LTC4, not LTB4.
- (D) **Epoxide Hydrolase:** This is an alternative name for LTA4 Hydrolase (LTA4H). Therefore, it suffers from the same incorrectness as option (B). It converts LTA4 to LTB4 in myeloid cells, not LTC4 in mast cells.
- (E) **Cyclooxygenase-2 (COX-2):** This enzyme is involved in the synthesis

<a id='question-033'></a>

### Question 33: Aspirin-Exacerbated Respiratory Disease (AERD) / Samter's Triad
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: The role of cysteinyl leukotrienes (LTC4, LTD4, LTE4) in inflammatory responses, specifically their synthesis via Leukotriene C4 synthase (LTC4S) involving glutathione conjugation, and their relevance in conditions like Aspirin-Exacerbated Respiratory Disease (AERD).

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 45-year-old male presents to the emergency department with acute onset shortness of breath, wheezing, and rhinorrhea following the ingestion of ibuprofen for a headache. He has a history of chronic rhinosinusitis with nasal polyps, asthma, and recurrent upper respiratory infections. He reports similar severe reactions after taking aspirin in the past. Physical examination reveals diffuse wheezing and stridor. Nasal endoscopy confirms multiple large, pale, edematous polyps obstructing the nasal passages. A biopsy of one of the nasal polyps is obtained for histological examination. Which of the following enzymatic steps is most likely upregulated in the inflammatory cells within this patient's nasal polyps, contributing to his symptoms?

(A) Phospholipase A2 activation, leading to arachidonic acid release from membrane phospholipids.
(B) 5-Lipoxygenase (5-LOX) converting arachidonic acid to leukotriene A4 (LTA4).
(C) Leukotriene C4 synthase (LTC4S) conjugating LTA4 with reduced glutathione (GSH).
(D) Cyclooxygenase (COX) converting arachidonic acid to prostaglandin H2 (PGH2).
(E) Lipoxygenase (LOX) converting LTA4 to LTB4.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 45-year-old male with asthma, chronic rhinosinusitis with nasal polyps, and aspirin sensitivity. This constellation of symptoms is classic for Aspirin-Exacerbated Respiratory Disease (AERD), also known as Samter's Triad.
- **Trigger:** Symptoms acutely worsen after ibuprofen ingestion. NSAIDs like ibuprofen and aspirin inhibit COX-1 and COX-2.
- **Mechanism in AERD:** In AERD patients, inhibition of COX enzymes by NSAIDs leads to a relative shunting of arachidonic acid metabolism towards the 5-lipoxygenase (5-LOX) pathway. This results in increased production of leukotrienes, particularly the cysteinyl leukotrienes (LTC4, LTD4, LTE4).
- **Inflammatory Cells:** Mast cells, eosinophils, and basophils are key players in the inflammatory response in AERD and asthma, residing within the nasal polyps and airways.
- **Leukotriene Synthesis:** The synthesis of cysteinyl leukotrienes involves several steps:
    1.  Phospholipase A2 releases arachidonic acid.
    2.  5-Lipoxygenase converts arachidonic acid to LTA4.
    3.  **Leukotriene C4 synthase (LTC4S)** conjugates LTA4 with reduced glutathione (GSH) to form LTC4. This step is crucial and often upregulated in AERD.
    4.  LTC4 is exported and further metabolized to LTD4 and LTE4.
- **Question Stem:** The question asks which enzymatic step is *most likely upregulated* in the inflammatory cells within the nasal polyps, contributing to the patient's symptoms. Given the pathophysiology of AERD, the increased production of cysteinyl leukotrienes due to 5-LOX pathway activation is central. The rate-limiting and specific step for cysteinyl leukotriene synthesis is the conjugation of LTA4 with GSH by LTC4S.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **Initiating Stimulus:** NSAID (ibuprofen) ingestion.
- **Molecular Event:** Inhibition of COX-1 and COX-2 enzymes.
- **Cellular Response:** Shunting of arachidonic acid metabolism from the cyclooxygenase pathway (producing prostaglandins and thromboxanes) to the lipoxygenase pathway.
- **Biochemical Cascade:**
    1.  Phospholipase A2 (PLA2) cleaves membrane phospholipids, releasing arachidonic acid. (Option A is an upstream step, but not the specific upregulated step leading to cysteinyl leukotrienes).
    2.  5-Lipoxygenase (5-LOX) converts arachidonic acid to LTA4 (leukotriene A4). (Option B is a key step, but the subsequent conjugation is the defining step for cysteinyl leukotrienes).
    3.  **Leukotriene C4 Synthase (LTC4S)**, a microsomal glutathione S-transferase, catalyzes the conjugation of LTA4 with reduced glutathione (GSH). This reaction forms Leukotriene C4 (LTC4). This step is crucial for the formation of cysteinyl leukotrienes, which are potent inflammatory mediators. In AERD, this enzyme activity is often upregulated, leading to excessive LTC4 production. (Option C is the specific enzyme responsible for forming the cysteinyl leukotrienes, which are central to AERD pathophysiology).
    4.  LTC4 is unstable and is either exported from the cell or further metabolized intracellularly. Extracellular LTC4 is converted to LTD4 (Leukotriene D4) by gamma-glutamyl transpeptidase and then to LTE4 (Leukotriene E4) by dipeptidases.
- **Cellular Source:** Mast cells, eosinophils, and basophils within the nasal polyps and airways are the primary producers of these leukotrienes in AERD.
- **Clinical Manifestation:** LTC4, LTD4, and LTE4 bind to specific receptors (CysLT1 and CysLT2) on airway smooth muscle cells, endothelial cells, and inflammatory cells, causing bronchoconstriction, increased vascular permeability, mucus secretion, and eosinophil recruitment – all contributing to the patient's symptoms of wheezing, shortness of breath, and rhinorrhea.
- **Conclusion:** The upregulation of LTC4S, leading to increased synthesis of cysteinyl leukotrienes, is a key pathogenic mechanism in AERD triggered by NSAID inhibition of COX pathways.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) Phospholipase A2 activation, leading to arachidonic acid release from membrane phospholipids. This is the initial step in both prostaglandin and leukotriene synthesis. While necessary, it's not the specific upregulated step characteristic of the leukotriene pathway shift in AERD. Arachidonic acid release is upstream and common to both pathways.
- (B) 5-Lipoxygenase (5-LOX) converting arachidonic acid to leukotriene A4 (LTA4). 5-LOX activity is indeed increased in AERD due to the shunting of arachidonic acid. However, the question asks for the step *most likely upregulated* specifically leading to cysteinyl leukotrienes. While 5-LOX is crucial, the subsequent conjugation step by LTC4S is the defining reaction for LTC4 formation and is often considered the rate-limiting step for cysteinyl leukotriene synthesis. Furthermore, LTA4 can also be converted to LTB4 by LTA4 hydrolase, which is not the primary mediator in AERD.
- (D) Cyclooxygenase (COX) converting arachidonic acid to prostaglandin H2 (PGH2). This is the first step in the prostaglandin pathway. In AERD, COX enzymes are *inhibited* by NSAIDs, leading to the observed symptoms. Therefore, this step is downregulated, not upregulated.
- (E) Lipoxygenase (LOX) converting LTA4 to LTB4. This refers to LTA4 hydrolase, which converts LTA4 to LTB4 (leukotriene B4). LTB4 is a potent chemoattractant for neutrophils and other leukocytes, but it is not a cysteinyl leukotriene and is not considered the primary mediator responsible for the bronchoconstriction and mucus hypersecretion seen in AERD. The synthesis of cysteinyl leukotrienes (LTC4

### Question 34: Resolution of Acute Inflammation
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Transcellular biosynthesis of lipoxins (LXA4, LXB4) involving neutrophil 5-lipoxygenase (5-LOX) and platelet 12-lipoxygenase (12-LOX) during the resolution phase of acute inflammation. (脂氧素 (LXA4, LXB4) 的跨细胞生物合成，涉及炎症消退期中性粒细胞 5-氧化酶 (5-LOX) 和血小板 12-氧化酶 (12-LOX))

#### Clinical Vignette:
A research team is investigating the mechanisms underlying the resolution of acute inflammation in a murine model of sterile peritonitis induced by intraperitoneal injection of zymosan. They observe that during the later stages (48-72 hours post-injection), the inflammatory response begins to subside, characterized by decreased neutrophil infiltration and reduced levels of pro-inflammatory cytokines like TNF-α and IL-1β. To elucidate the biochemical pathways involved, they perform experiments using genetically modified mice.

In one experiment, they use mice deficient in 5-lipoxygenase (5-LOX) in neutrophils. These mice exhibit prolonged neutrophil accumulation in the peritoneal cavity and delayed resolution of inflammation compared to wild-type controls. In a separate experiment, they use mice deficient in 12-lipoxygenase (12-LOX) in platelets. These mice show impaired production of specialized pro-resolving mediators (SPMs) and also exhibit delayed resolution of inflammation, although the effect is less pronounced than in the 5-LOX deficient neutrophils.

Further analysis reveals that neutrophils, upon activation by zymosan, produce leukotriene A4 (LTA4) via the 5-LOX pathway. Adherent platelets, which lack 5-LOX but possess high levels of 12-LOX, are found in close proximity to activated neutrophils within the inflamed peritoneum. The researchers hypothesize that LTA4 produced by neutrophils is transferred to platelets, where it serves as a substrate for platelet 12-LOX, leading to the synthesis of lipoxin A4 (LXA4). They also find evidence for the production of lipoxin B4 (LXB4), which requires both 5-LOX and 12-LOX activity, suggesting a similar transcellular mechanism involving neutrophil 5-LOX producing LTA4 and platelet 12-LOX converting it to LXA4, which can then be further metabolized or act directly.

Which of the following biochemical pathways BEST explains the transcellular biosynthesis of lipoxin A4 (LXA4) during the resolution phase of acute inflammation in this model?

(A) Platelet 5-LOX converts arachidonic acid directly into LTA4, which is then converted to LXA4.
(B) Neutrophil 12-LOX converts LTA4 into LXA4.
(C) Neutrophil 5-LOX converts arachidonic acid into LTA4, which is then transferred to platelets and converted into LXA4 by platelet 12-LOX.
(D) Platelet 5-LOX converts LTA4 (produced by other cells) into LXA4.
(E) Neutrophil 12-LOX converts arachidonic acid into LTA4, which is then converted to LXA4.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Setting:** Research study on resolution of acute inflammation (sterile peritonitis model).
- **Key Observation:** Resolution phase (48-72h) involves decreased neutrophil infiltration and pro-inflammatory cytokines.
- **Experimental Findings:**
    - Neutrophil 5-LOX deficiency -> prolonged inflammation.
    - Platelet 12-LOX deficiency -> impaired SPM production, delayed resolution (less severe).
- **Biochemical Pathway:**
    - Neutrophils produce LTA4 via 5-LOX upon activation.
    - Platelets lack 5-LOX but have high 12-LOX.
    - Platelets are near activated neutrophils.
    - Hypothesis: Neutrophil LTA4 transferred to platelets -> Platelet 12-LOX converts LTA4 -> LXA4.
    - LXB4 also produced, requiring both 5-LOX and 12-LOX.
- **Question:** Mechanism of LXA4 transcellular biosynthesis.

The key clues point towards a cooperative mechanism between neutrophils and platelets. Neutrophils initiate the pathway using 5-LOX to produce LTA4. Platelets, lacking 5-LOX but possessing 12-LOX, receive LTA4 from neutrophils and convert it into LXA4. This transcellular process is crucial for the resolution phase.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **Initiation:** Inflammation (e.g., zymosan injection) activates neutrophils.
- **Neutrophil Step:** Activated neutrophils express and utilize 5-lipoxygenase (5-LOX). 5-LOX acts on arachidonic acid (released from membrane phospholipids by phospholipase A2) to produce leukotriene A4 (LTA4).
- **Transcellular Transfer:** LTA4 is unstable and can be further metabolized by neutrophil 5-LOX into other leukotrienes (LTB4, LTC4, LTD4, LTE4) or by LTA4 hydrolase into LTB4. However, in the context of resolution, LTA4 can also be transferred to adjacent cells, specifically platelets.
- **Platelet Step:** Platelets adhere to the endothelium during inflammation. They lack functional 5-LOX but express high levels of 12-lipoxygenase (12-LOX). Upon receiving LTA4 from neutrophils, platelet 12-LOX acts on LTA4.
- **LXA4 Synthesis:** Platelet 12-LOX converts LTA4 into 12-hydroxy-LTA4, which is then converted to lipoxin A4 (LXA4) via further enzymatic steps (e.g., oxidation, dehydration).
- **LXB4 Synthesis:** Lipoxin B4 (LXB4) synthesis also requires both 5-LOX and 12-LOX. It is thought that neutrophil 5-LOX produces LTA4, which is then transferred to platelets. Platelet 12-LOX converts LTA4 to 12-HETE, which can then react with LTA4 (either from the neutrophil or transferred to the platelet) to form LXB4. Alternatively, some evidence suggests LXB4 synthesis might occur directly within neutrophils via a complex pathway involving both enzymes. However, the transcellular route involving platelet 12-LOX is a major pathway for LXA4 and contributes to LXB4 production.
- **Resolution:** LXA4 and LXB4 are specialized pro-resolving mediators (SPMs). They promote the resolution of inflammation by inhibiting neutrophil recruitment, stimulating macrophage phagocytosis of apoptotic neutrophils (efferocytosis), and suppressing pro-inflammatory cytokine production.
- **Experimental Confirmation:** The vignette explicitly states that neutrophil 5-LOX deficiency impairs LTA4 production, leading to reduced LXA4/LXB4 synthesis and delayed resolution. Platelet 12-LOX deficiency also impairs LXA4/LXB4 synthesis and delays resolution, confirming the essential role of both enzymes in this transcellular pathway.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): **Incorrect.** Platelets lack 5-LOX. They cannot convert arachidonic acid directly to LTA4 via 5-LOX. This option misattributes the 5-LOX activity to platelets.
- (B): **Incorrect.** Neutrophils lack significant 12-LOX activity. While they produce LTA4 via 5-LOX, they do not convert LTA4 to LXA4 using 12-LOX. This option misattributes the 12-LOX activity to neutrophils in this specific conversion step.
- (D): **Incorrect.** Platelets lack 5-LOX. They cannot convert LTA4 (produced by *other

### Question 35: Inflammation Resolution
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Lipoxins (LXA4, LXB4) are endogenous lipid mediators derived from arachidonic acid that actively promote the resolution of inflammation by inhibiting neutrophil recruitment and function while stimulating macrophage phagocytosis of apoptotic cells. (Lipoxins (LXA4, LXB4) 是从花生四烯酸衍生的内源性脂质介质，它们通过抑制中性粒细胞募集和功能，同时刺激巨噬细胞吞噬凋亡细胞，从而主动促进炎症消退。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 45-year-old male presents to the emergency department three days after sustaining a deep laceration on his forearm during a gardening accident. The wound initially showed signs of acute inflammation: redness, swelling, warmth, and pain. However, over the past 24 hours, the redness and swelling have significantly decreased, the warmth has resolved, and the pain is now minimal. The wound site is covered with a thin layer of serous fluid and several scattered, non-motile neutrophils undergoing phagocytosis by macrophages. An in vitro assay is performed using human leukocytes. Researchers observe that adding a specific lipid mediator to the assay culture medium significantly reduces the rolling and adhesion of neutrophils on endothelial cells stimulated with TNF-α. Simultaneously, the same mediator enhances the recruitment and phagocytic activity of monocytes towards apoptotic neutrophils. Which of the following best describes the primary mechanism of action of this lipid mediator in resolving inflammation?

(A) Activation of phospholipase A2 (PLA2) leading to increased production of pro-inflammatory eicosanoids like leukotrienes and prostaglandins.
(B) Inhibition of cyclooxygenase (COX) enzymes, thereby reducing the synthesis of pro-inflammatory prostaglandins involved in vasodilation and pain.
(C) Binding to G protein-coupled receptors (GPCRs) on leukocytes, suppressing neutrophil chemotaxis and adhesion while promoting macrophage phagocytosis of apoptotic cells.
(D) Induction of caspase-dependent apoptosis in neutrophils, leading to their rapid clearance and termination of the inflammatory response.
(E) Upregulation of nuclear factor kappa B (NF-κB) signaling, promoting the transcription of genes encoding anti-inflammatory cytokines like IL-10.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** A 45-year-old male with a resolving wound infection. The initial acute inflammation (redness, swelling, warmth, pain) is subsiding, indicating the transition from the pro-inflammatory phase to the resolution phase.
- **Wound Examination:** Decreased redness/swelling, resolved warmth, minimal pain. Presence of serous fluid, non-motile neutrophils being phagocytosed by macrophages. These findings are characteristic of the resolution phase of inflammation.
- **In Vitro Assay:** A lipid mediator is added, causing:
    - Decreased neutrophil rolling and adhesion on TNF-α stimulated endothelium.
    - Enhanced monocyte recruitment and phagocytosis of apoptotic neutrophils.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the primary mechanism of action of this lipid mediator in resolving inflammation.
- **Key Clues Pointing to Lipoxins:** The observed effects (suppression of neutrophil recruitment/function, stimulation of macrophage phagocytosis) are the hallmark actions of lipoxins (LXA4 and LXB4), which are endogenous resolving mediators. The transition from acute inflammation to resolution in the clinical vignette strongly suggests the involvement of such mediators.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **Inflammation Resolution:** Acute inflammation is a tightly regulated process involving initiation, amplification, and resolution. Resolution is an active process, not just passive decay.
- **Lipoxins (LXA4, LXB4):** These are specialized pro-resolving mediators (SPMs) derived from arachidonic acid via the lipoxygenase pathway (specifically 5-LOX and 15-LOX). They are generated during the resolution phase of inflammation.
- **Mechanism of Action:**
    - **Source:** LXA4 is primarily produced by leukocytes (neutrophils, monocytes), while LXB4 is produced by platelets.
    - **Receptors:** Lipoxins exert their effects by binding to specific G protein-coupled receptors (GPCRs) on leukocytes, primarily the ALX/FPR2 receptor.
    - **Effects on Neutrophils:** Binding to ALX/FPR2 on neutrophils inhibits their chemotaxis (migration towards the site of inflammation), adhesion to endothelial cells (rolling and firm adhesion), and transendothelial migration. This reduces the influx of new neutrophils into the inflamed tissue.
    - **Effects on Monocytes/Macrophages:** Lipoxins stimulate the recruitment of monocytes to the site of inflammation. They also enhance the phagocytic activity of macrophages, specifically promoting the clearance of apoptotic neutrophils (efferocytosis). Efferocytosis is crucial for resolution as it removes apoptotic cells, which can otherwise release pro-inflammatory contents.
    - **Overall Effect:** Lipoxins act as "braking signals" for neutrophil activity and "accelerators" for macrophage clearance, thereby actively orchestrating the resolution of inflammation and tissue repair.
- **Relevance to Vignette:** The lipid mediator described in the vignette perfectly matches the actions of lipoxins: suppressing neutrophil rolling/adhesion and enhancing monocyte recruitment/phagocytosis, all occurring during the resolution phase of the wound healing process.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): **Activation of phospholipase A2 (PLA2) leading to increased production of pro-inflammatory eicosanoids like leukotrienes and prostaglandins.** PLA2 activation releases arachidonic acid, which is the precursor for *both* pro-inflammatory mediators (like leukotrienes, prostaglandins) and anti-inflammatory/resolving mediators (like lipoxins, resolvins, protectins). However, the *primary* action described in the vignette (suppressing neutrophil function, promoting macrophage clearance) is characteristic of lipoxins, not the overall effect of PLA2 activation, which would also increase pro-inflammatory mediators. This option describes the *initiation* of eicosanoid synthesis, not the specific resolving mechanism.
- (B): **Inhibition of cyclooxygenase (COX) enzymes, thereby reducing the synthesis of pro-inflammatory prostaglandins involved in vasodilation and pain.** COX inhibition is the mechanism of action for NSAIDs (like aspirin, ibuprofen). While reducing prostaglandins can decrease inflammation signs (vasodilation, pain), it doesn't specifically explain the suppression of neutrophil rolling/adhesion and the stimulation of macrophage phagocytosis described in the vignette. This mechanism primarily targets prostaglandin synthesis, not the specific actions of lipoxins on leukocyte behavior.
- (D): **Induction of caspase-dependent apoptosis in neutrophils, leading to their rapid clearance and termination of the inflammatory response.** Neutrophils *do* undergo apoptosis during inflammation, and their clearance by macrophages is essential for resolution. However, lipoxins primarily *enhance* the phagocytosis of *already apoptotic* neutrophils; they don't directly induce apoptosis. While apoptosis is part of resolution, this option misattributes the direct action of lipoxins. Furthermore, the vignette describes non-motile neutrophils being phagocytosed, suggesting they are already undergoing apoptosis or are apoptotic bodies.
- (E): **Upregulation of nuclear factor kappa B (NF-κB) signaling, promoting the transcription of genes encoding anti-inflammatory cytokines like IL-10.** NF-κB is a *pro-inflammatory* transcription factor. Its activation leads to the production of pro-inflammatory cytokines (like TNF-α, IL-1β, IL-6). While IL-10 is an anti-inflammatory cytokine, its production is typically *downregulated* or counteracted during the resolution phase. Lipoxins, conversely, often act downstream or independently of NF-κB signaling to promote resolution. This option describes a pro-inflammatory mechanism leading to an anti-inflammatory outcome, which is incorrect for the primary action of lipoxins.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- **High-Yield Takeaway:** Lipoxins (LXA4, LXB4) are endogenous lipid mediators crucial for actively resolving acute inflammation by inhibiting neutrophil recruitment/function and promoting macrophage phagocytosis of apoptotic cells. They bind to GPCRs (like ALX/FPR2) on leukocytes.
- **Differentials:** Understand the difference between pro-inflammatory

### Question 36: Aspirin-Triggered Lipoxins
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Aspirin's mechanism of action involves acetylation of cyclooxygenase (COX) enzymes. While it inhibits COX-1 and COX-2's prostaglandin synthesis, it also induces a switch in COX-2 activity towards 15-lipoxygenase, leading to the production of anti-inflammatory lipoxins. (阿司匹林的药理作用机制涉及环氧化酶（COX）的乙酰化。虽然它抑制COX-1和COX-2的前列腺素合成，但它还会诱导COX-2活性向15-脂氧化酶转变，从而产生抗炎脂氧素。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old male with a history of stable angina pectoris is prescribed low-dose aspirin (81 mg daily) for secondary prevention of cardiovascular events. During a routine laboratory evaluation, his neutrophil function is assessed following an in vitro challenge with lipopolysaccharide (LPS). Compared to controls, his neutrophils exhibit significantly reduced production of leukotriene B4 (LTB4) and decreased migration towards an inflammatory site, despite normal levels of arachidonic acid metabolites like prostaglandin E2 (PGE2) and thromboxane A2 (TXA2). Further analysis reveals increased levels of 15-hydroxyeicosatetraenoic acid (15-HETE) and 15-epi-lipoxilin A4 (15-epi-LXA4) in the neutrophil supernatant. Which of the following mechanisms best explains these findings?

(A) Aspirin irreversibly inhibits both COX-1 and COX-2, leading to decreased production of all arachidonic acid metabolites, including prostaglandins, thromboxanes, and leukotrienes.
(B) Aspirin selectively inhibits COX-1, reducing thromboxane A2 production in platelets but having minimal effect on COX-2 activity in neutrophils, which primarily produces LTB4 via the 5-lipoxygenase pathway.
(C) Aspirin acetylates COX-2, shifting its enzymatic activity from cyclooxygenase to 15-lipoxygenase, promoting the synthesis of 15-HETE, which is then converted by neutrophil 5-lipoxygenase into anti-inflammatory lipoxins like 15-epi-LXA4.
(D) Aspirin inhibits phospholipase A2 (PLA2), reducing the availability of arachidonic acid, thereby decreasing the synthesis of all eicosanoids, including prostaglandins, thromboxanes, leukotrienes, and lipoxins.
(E) Aspirin stimulates the production of cortisol, which suppresses the inflammatory response by inhibiting the transcription of genes encoding inflammatory mediators like COX-2 and 5-lipoxygenase.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 62-year-old male with stable angina, taking low-dose aspirin. This sets the context for aspirin's effects.
- **Experimental Setup:** In vitro neutrophil challenge with LPS. This tests the inflammatory response pathway.
- **Key Findings:**
    - Reduced LTB4 production: Indicates suppression of the 5-lipoxygenase pathway.
    - Decreased neutrophil migration: A consequence of reduced LTB4 (a chemoattractant).
    - Normal PGE2 and TXA2 levels: Suggests COX inhibition is not complete or is modified.
    - Increased 15-HETE and 15-epi-LXA4: Points towards a specific shift in COX-2 activity towards 15-lipoxygenase.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the mechanism explaining these specific findings (reduced LTB4, normal PGE2/TXA2, increased 15-HETE/15-epi-LXA4).

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **Aspirin's Primary Action:** Aspirin irreversibly acetylates a serine residue (Ser530 in COX-1, Ser516 in COX-2) within the active site of both COX-1 and COX-2. This blocks the access of arachidonic acid to the cyclooxygenase active site, inhibiting prostaglandin and thromboxane synthesis.
- **Aspirin's Secondary Action (Focus of the Question):** At low doses, aspirin can acetylate Ser516 in COX-2. This acetylation *does not* completely abolish COX-2 activity. Instead, it shifts the enzyme's preference from cyclooxygenase activity (producing PGG2/PGH2, precursors to PGE2, PGI2, TXA2) towards 15-lipoxygenase activity.
- **15-Lipoxygenase Pathway:** Acetylated COX-2 (specifically, the 15-lipoxygenase form) converts arachidonic acid into 15-HETE.
- **Lipoxin Synthesis:** 15-HETE is a substrate for neutrophil 5-lipoxygenase (5-LOX). 5-LOX acts on 15-HETE to produce lipoxins, specifically 15-epi-LXA4 (ATL - Aspirin-Triggered Lipoxin).
- **Lipoxins' Role:** Lipoxins (like 15-epi-LXA4) are potent anti-inflammatory and pro-resolution mediators. They inhibit neutrophil chemotaxis (migration), reduce LTB4 production, and promote the clearance of inflammatory cells.
- **Connecting to the Vignette:** The patient taking aspirin shows reduced LTB4 production and decreased neutrophil migration (consistent with lipoxin effects). Normal PGE2/TXA2 suggests the *cyclooxygenase* activity of COX-2 is partially inhibited but not completely silenced, and the shift to 15-lipoxygenase activity is occurring. The increased 15-HETE and 15-epi-LXA4 directly confirm the activation of this alternative pathway. Therefore, the mechanism involves aspirin acetylating COX-2, shifting its activity to 15-lipoxygenase, leading to lipoxin production.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Incorrect. Aspirin *does* inhibit COX-1 and COX-2, reducing prostaglandins and thromboxanes. However, the vignette specifically mentions *normal* PGE2/TXA2 levels, contradicting complete inhibition. Furthermore, it doesn't explain the increased 15-HETE/15-epi-LXA4 or the reduced LTB4 (lipoxins inhibit LTB4 production). Aspirin's effect is more nuanced than simple complete inhibition.
- (B): Incorrect. Aspirin inhibits *both* COX-1 and COX-2, although its effect on COX-1 is more pronounced and longer-lasting, especially at low doses. The key finding here is the *shift* in COX-2 activity, not just selective COX-1 inhibition. Also, this option doesn't explain the increased 15-HETE/15-epi-LXA4.
- (D): Incorrect. Aspirin's primary target is COX, not PLA2. PLA2 inhibition would reduce *all* arachidonic acid metabolites, including prostaglandins, thromboxanes, leukotrienes, and lipoxins. The vignette shows normal PGE2/TXA2 and increased lipoxins, ruling out PLA2 inhibition.
- (E): Incorrect. Aspirin does not directly stimulate cortisol production. Cortisol is a glucocorticoid hormone produced by the adrenal glands, which has broad anti-inflammatory effects, including suppressing COX-2 and 5-LOX gene expression. Aspirin's mechanism is direct enzymatic modification, not hormonal stimulation.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- **High-Yield Takeaway:** Aspirin's anti-inflammatory effects are not solely due to inhibiting prostaglandin synthesis. At low doses, it can induce a switch in COX-2 activity towards 15-lipoxygenase, leading to the production of anti-inflammatory lipoxins (15-epi-LXA4). This is a key example of aspirin's pleiotropic effects.
- **Differentials:** Understand the difference between COX-1 vs. COX-2 inhibition (platelet vs. inflammation), the role of different lipoxygenases (5-LOX vs. 12-LOX vs.

### Question 37: Inflammation Resolution & Lipid Mediator Pathways
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Specialized pro-resolving mediators (SPMs) derived from omega-3 fatty acids (EPA and DHA) actively resolve inflammation by promoting neutrophil apoptosis, efferocytosis, and tissue repair. (炎症消退与脂质介体途径：源自omega-3脂肪酸（EPA和DHA）的特化促消退介体（SPMs）通过促进中性粒细胞凋亡、吞噬和组织修复来主动消退炎症。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old male presents to the emergency department with a 3-day history of worsening right lower quadrant abdominal pain, nausea, and low-grade fever (38.0°C). He has a history of hypertension and hyperlipidemia, managed with lisinopril and atorvastatin, respectively. Physical examination reveals localized tenderness and guarding in the right lower quadrant, with rebound tenderness. Laboratory results show leukocytosis (18,000/µL) with neutrophilia (85%) and elevated C-reactive protein (CRP). A CT scan confirms acute appendicitis with localized peritonitis. The patient undergoes an emergency appendectomy. Post-operatively, analysis of the exudate collected from the surgical site reveals a significant increase in lipid mediators derived from omega-3 fatty acids, specifically docosahexaenoic acid (DHA). These mediators are found to enhance the phagocytosis of apoptotic neutrophils by macrophages and stimulate tissue regeneration in the inflamed appendiceal bed. Which of the following classes of lipid mediators is MOST likely responsible for these pro-resolving effects observed in the post-operative exudate?

(A) Leukotrienes derived from arachidonic acid via the 5-lipoxygenase pathway.
(B) Prostaglandins derived from arachidonic acid via the cyclooxygenase pathway.
(C) Eicosanoids derived from eicosapentaenoic acid (EPA) via the 5-lipoxygenase pathway.
(D) Specialized pro-resolving mediators (SPMs) derived from docosahexaenoic acid (DHA) via 12- and 15-lipoxygenase pathways.
(E) Thromboxanes derived from arachidonic acid via the cyclooxygenase pathway.

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 62-year-old male with acute appendicitis and localized peritonitis, presenting classic signs/symptoms (RLQ pain, fever, leukocytosis, elevated CRP, CT findings). This establishes an acute inflammatory process.
- **Post-operative Exudate Analysis:** The key finding is the analysis of exudate from the surgical site, revealing increased lipid mediators derived from omega-3 fatty acid DHA.
- **Mediator Function:** These mediators are described as enhancing phagocytosis of apoptotic neutrophils (efferocytosis) and stimulating tissue regeneration. These are hallmark functions of inflammation resolution.
- **Question Stem:** The question asks to identify the class of lipid mediators responsible for these pro-resolving effects, specifically linked to DHA.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **Inflammation Resolution:** Acute inflammation is a tightly regulated process involving initiation, propagation, and resolution. Resolution is an active process, not just passive decline.
- **Omega-3 Fatty Acids:** EPA and DHA are omega-3 polyunsaturated fatty acids (PUFAs) known for their anti-inflammatory and pro-resolving properties.
- **SPMs:** EPA and DHA are precursors for Specialized Pro-Resolving Mediators (SPMs).
    - EPA is metabolized via 5-lipoxygenase (5-LOX) to produce E-series resolvins (RvE1, RvE2, RvE3).
    - DHA is metabolized via 12-LOX and 15-LOX pathways to produce D-series resolvins (RvD1-RvD6), protectins (PD1, PD2, NPD1), and maresins (MaR1).
- **Mechanism of Action:** SPMs actively promote the resolution of inflammation by:
    - Inhibiting neutrophil recruitment.
    - Stimulating neutrophil apoptosis.
    - Enhancing the clearance of apoptotic neutrophils by macrophages (efferocytosis) via receptors like GPR18 and GPR32.
    - Promoting tissue regeneration.
- **Vignette Connection:** The vignette describes exudate analysis showing mediators derived from DHA that enhance neutrophil clearance and tissue regeneration. This perfectly matches the known functions and origins of SPMs derived from DHA (RvDs, Protectins, Maresins).

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Leukotrienes:** Derived from arachidonic acid (an omega-6 fatty acid) via 5-LOX. They are potent pro-inflammatory mediators involved in bronchoconstriction, increased vascular permeability, and chemotaxis (e.g., LTB4). They *promote* inflammation, not resolve it. Incorrect.
- (B) **Prostaglandins:** Derived from arachidonic acid via cyclooxygenase (COX) pathways. They mediate pain, fever, vasodilation, and inflammation (e.g., PGE2). They are primarily pro-inflammatory. Incorrect.
- (C) **Eicosanoids derived from EPA:** EPA is metabolized via 5-LOX to produce E-series resolvins (RvE1, RvE2, RvE3). While these are SPMs and pro-resolving, the vignette specifically mentions mediators derived from *DHA*. This option is partially correct regarding the class (SPMs) but incorrect regarding the specific fatty acid precursor mentioned in the vignette. Incorrect.
- (E) **Thromboxanes:** Derived from arachidonic acid via COX pathways. Primarily involved in platelet aggregation and vasoconstriction (e.g., TXA2). They are not directly involved in the resolution phase described. Incorrect.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- **High-Yield Takeaway:** Understand the role of omega-3 fatty acids (EPA and DHA) as precursors to Specialized Pro-Resolving Mediators (SPMs) that actively resolve inflammation by promoting neutrophil clearance and tissue repair. Differentiate SPMs from pro-inflammatory eicosanoids derived from arachidonic acid (omega-6).
- **Differentials:** Remember that EPA yields E-series resolvins, while DHA yields D-series resolvins, protectins, and maresins. Distinguish SPMs from other lipid mediators like leukotrienes, prostaglandins, and thromboxanes, which are primarily pro-inflammatory. Also, recall that SPMs act on specific G protein-coupled receptors (GPCRs) like GPR18 and GPR32 to exert their effects.

<a id='question-038'></a>

### Question 38: Platelet Aggregation & Leukocyte Recruitment in Acute Microvascular Injury
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: The role of the 12-lipoxygenase (12-LOX) pathway in platelets, specifically the production of 12-HETE and hepoxilins, in mediating platelet aggregation and leukocyte recruitment following acute microvascular injury. (血小板12-脂氧化酶通路在血小板聚集和白细胞募集中的作用)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old male with a history of stable angina presents to the emergency department complaining of sudden onset, severe chest pain radiating to his left arm. An electrocardiogram (ECG) shows ST-segment elevation in leads II, III, and aVF, consistent with an acute inferior myocardial infarction. He is immediately taken to the cardiac catheterization lab, where angiography reveals a complete occlusion of the right coronary artery (RCA) due to a ruptured atherosclerotic plaque. During percutaneous coronary intervention (PCI) with stent placement, the patient experiences acute thrombotic occlusion of a distal microvascular branch within the infarct zone. Microscopic analysis of the occluding thrombus reveals a high concentration of activated platelets and adherent leukocytes. Researchers are investigating the specific lipid mediators involved in this acute microvascular thrombosis. They find that inhibiting platelet 12-lipoxygenase (12-LOX) significantly reduces both platelet aggregation within the microthrombus and the adhesion of neutrophils to the injured endothelial surface at the site of occlusion. Which of the following lipid mediators is MOST likely responsible for the observed effects of 12-LOX inhibition on platelet aggregation and leukocyte recruitment in this scenario?

(A) Thromboxane A2 (TXA2)
(B) Prostaglandin E2 (PGE2)
(C) 12-Hydroperoxyeicosatetraenoic acid (12-HPETE)
(D) 20-Hydroperoxyeicosatetraenoic acid (20-HPETE)
(E) 12-Hydroxyeicosatetraenoic acid (12-HETE)

#### Correct Answer: E

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 62M with acute inferior MI (ST elevation in II, III, aVF) due to RCA occlusion by ruptured plaque. This establishes the context of acute coronary thrombosis.
- **Complication:** Acute thrombotic occlusion of a distal microvascular branch during PCI. This focuses the question on microvascular thrombosis, a common complication of MI.
- **Thrombus Composition:** High concentration of activated platelets and adherent leukocytes. This highlights the key cellular players involved.
- **Experimental Finding:** Inhibition of platelet 12-LOX reduces platelet aggregation and neutrophil adhesion in the microthrombus. This is the central finding linking 12-LOX to the pathophysiology.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the lipid mediator MOST likely responsible for the effects of 12-LOX inhibition. This requires identifying the downstream product(s) of 12-LOX involved in platelet aggregation and leukocyte recruitment.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **Initiating Event:** Rupture of an atherosclerotic plaque exposes thrombogenic material (collagen, tissue factor) to blood.
- **Platelet Activation:** Exposed collagen binds to platelet GPVI and GPIb receptors, activating platelets. Tissue factor initiates the coagulation cascade.
- **Arachidonic Acid Metabolism:** Activated platelets release arachidonic acid (AA) from membrane phospholipids via phospholipase A2 (PLA2). AA is metabolized by cyclooxygenase (COX) and lipoxygenases (LOX).
- **COX Pathway:** COX-1 produces prostaglandin H2 (PGH2), which is converted to TXA2 by thromboxane synthase. TXA2 is a potent platelet aggregator and vasoconstrictor.
- **LOX Pathways:**
    - **5-LOX:** Produces leukotrienes (e.g., LTB4), primarily involved in leukocyte chemotaxis and activation.
    - **12-LOX:** Found in platelets and other cells. Metabolizes AA to 12-HPETE and 12-HETE. 12-HETE is a potent platelet aggregator, potentially acting via G-protein coupled receptors (GPCRs) like PAR-1 and PAR-4, and also promotes leukocyte adhesion to endothelial cells. Hepoxilins (derived from 12-HPETE) also contribute to platelet aggregation and leukocyte recruitment.
    - **15-LOX:** Produces lipoxins, which are generally anti-inflammatory and inhibit platelet aggregation.
- **Role of 12-LOX in Microthrombosis:** The vignette states that inhibiting 12-LOX reduces platelet aggregation and neutrophil adhesion. This directly implicates the products of 12-LOX (12-HETE and hepoxilins) as mediators of these processes in the context of acute microvascular injury. 12-HETE promotes platelet aggregation and adhesion, and also enhances neutrophil adhesion to endothelial cells, contributing significantly to thrombus formation and inflammation in the microvasculature.
- **Conclusion:** Since inhibiting 12-LOX reduces platelet aggregation and leukocyte recruitment, the products of 12-LOX, primarily 12-HETE and hepoxilins, are responsible for these effects. Among the options, 12-HETE is listed.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) Thromboxane A2 (TXA2): TXA2 is produced via the COX pathway (COX-1). While it is a potent platelet aggregator and vasoconstrictor crucial in thrombosis, inhibiting 12-LOX would not directly affect TXA2 production. Aspirin inhibits COX, not 12-LOX. Therefore, TXA2 is not the mediator whose effects are blocked by 12-LOX inhibition.
- (B) Prostaglandin E2 (PGE2): PGE2 is also produced via the COX pathway (COX-1 and COX-2). It generally inhibits platelet aggregation and causes vasodilation. Inhibiting 12-LOX would not affect PGE2 production.
- (C) 12-Hydroperoxyeicosatetraenoic acid (12-HPETE): 12-HPETE is an intermediate in the 12-LOX pathway, being converted to 12-HETE and hepoxilins. While it's part of the pathway, 12-HETE and hepoxilins are considered the more direct mediators of the observed biological effects (platelet aggregation, leukocyte recruitment). 12-HPETE itself is less stable and less studied as a direct effector molecule compared to 12-HETE. The question asks for the mediator *responsible* for the effects, and 12-HETE is the primary bioactive metabolite.
- (D) 20-Hydroperoxyeicosatetraenoic acid (20-HPETE): 20-HPETE is an intermediate in the 5-LOX pathway, leading to leukotriene production. Inhibiting 12-LOX would not affect the 5-LOX pathway or 20-HPETE production.
- (E) 12-Hydroxyeicosatetraenoic acid (12-HETE): 12-HETE is a direct product of the 12-LOX enzyme acting on arachidonic acid. It is known to be a potent platelet aggregator and promoter of leukocyte adhesion. The vignette explicitly states that inhibiting 12-LOX reduces platelet aggregation and leukocyte recruitment, directly implicating the products of this enzyme, with 12-HETE being the primary bioactive metabolite listed among the options.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- **High-Yield Takeaway:** Understand the distinct roles of arachidonic acid metabolites produced via COX (TXA2, PGF2α, PGE2, PGI2) and LOX pathways (5-HETE/LTs, 12-HETE/Hepoxilins, 15

<a id='question-039'></a>

### Question 39: Viral Bronchitis Resolution & Lipid Mediator Switching
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: The resolution phase of inflammation involves switching from pro-inflammatory lipid mediators (like leukotrienes) to pro-resolving lipid mediators (like lipoxins, resolvins, protectins, maresins). This switch is mediated by specific enzymes, including 15-lipoxygenase (15-LOX), which generates 15-HETE, a precursor for specialized pro-resolving mediators (SPMs). (炎症消退期涉及从促炎性脂质介质（如白三烯）到促消退性脂质介质（如脂氧素、分解素、保护素、马瑞素）的转变。这种转变由特定酶介导，包括15-脂氧合酶（15-LOX），它产生15-HETE，这是专门促消退性介质（SPM）的前体。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 32-year-old previously healthy female presents to her primary care physician 7 days after the onset of symptoms consistent with a viral upper respiratory infection, including cough, rhinorrhea, and mild fever. Her symptoms have significantly improved over the past 3 days, with the cough becoming less frequent and productive, and the fever resolving. A bronchial brushing performed during this recovery phase reveals an increased number of reticulocytes and airway epithelial cells expressing elevated levels of 15-lipoxygenase-1 (15-LOX-1). Analysis of bronchoalveolar lavage fluid shows decreased levels of leukotriene B4 (LTB4) and increased levels of lipoxin A4 (LXA4). Which of the following enzymatic pathways is MOST directly responsible for initiating the switch from pro-inflammatory to pro-resolving lipid mediators observed in this patient?

(A) Cyclooxygenase-2 (COX-2) converting arachidonic acid to prostaglandin H2 (PGH2).
(B) 5-Lipoxygenase (5-LOX) converting arachidonic acid to leukotriene A4 (LTA4).
(C) 12-Lipoxygenase (12-LOX) converting arachidonic acid to 12-HETE.
(D) 15-Lipoxygenase (15-LOX) converting arachidonic acid to 15-HETE.
(E) Phospholipase A2 (PLA2) releasing arachidonic acid from membrane phospholipids.

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 32-year-old female recovering from viral bronchitis (symptoms improving). This sets the stage for the *resolution phase* of inflammation.
- **Bronchial Brushing Findings:** Increased reticulocytes and airway epithelial cells expressing elevated 15-LOX-1. This points to a specific enzyme involved in the resolution process within these cells.
- **Bronchoalveolar Lavage (BAL) Fluid Findings:** Decreased LTB4 (a pro-inflammatory leukotriene derived from 5-LOX) and increased LXA4 (a pro-resolving lipoxin derived from 15-LOX or 5-LOX pathways). This demonstrates the *switch* from pro-inflammatory to pro-resolving mediators.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the enzymatic pathway *most directly responsible* for initiating this switch.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **Inflammation Resolution:** The resolution of inflammation is an active process, not just a passive decline. It involves the production of specialized pro-resolving mediators (SPMs).
- **Lipid Mediator Classes:** Key lipid mediators include eicosanoids derived from arachidonic acid via cyclooxygenase (COX) and lipoxygenase (LOX) pathways.
    - **Pro-inflammatory:** Prostaglandins (via COX), Leukotrienes (via 5-LOX), Thromboxanes (via COX). LTB4 is a potent chemoattractant for neutrophils.
    - **Pro-resolving:** Lipoxins (LXs), Resolvins (Rv), Protectins (PD), Maresins (MaR). These promote neutrophil apoptosis, efferocytosis (phagocytosis of apoptotic cells), and tissue repair.
- **Enzyme Roles:**
    - **PLA2:** Releases arachidonic acid (AA) from membrane phospholipids. It's the *initiating* step but not specific to the switch.
    - **COX:** Produces PGH2, leading to prostaglandins and thromboxanes. Primarily pro-inflammatory.
    - **5-LOX:** Produces LTA4, which can be converted to LTB4 (pro-inflammatory) or LTD4/LTE4 (involved in bronchoconstriction).
    - **15-LOX:** Produces 15-HETE. 15-HETE can be further metabolized to SPMs like LXA4 (often via interaction with 5-LOX pathway intermediates) or can inhibit 5-LOX activity, thus reducing LTB4 production. Upregulation of 15-LOX during resolution is a key mechanism for generating SPMs and dampening pro-inflammatory signals.
    - **12-LOX:** Produces 12-HETE, primarily involved in platelet aggregation and other functions, less central to the pro-inflammatory/pro-resolving switch in this context.
- **Mechanism in Vignette:** The patient is in the resolution phase. 15-LOX-1 is upregulated in relevant cells. LTB4 (pro-inflammatory) decreases, and LXA4 (pro-resolving) increases. This directly links the activity of 15-LOX (producing 15-HETE, a precursor/intermediate in SPM synthesis and potentially inhibiting 5-LOX) to the observed switch in lipid mediator profiles. Therefore, 15-LOX is the enzyme most directly responsible for initiating this switch.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Cyclooxygenase-2 (COX-2):** COX enzymes (COX-1 and COX-2) produce prostaglandins and thromboxanes. While involved in inflammation, they are primarily associated with pro-inflammatory effects (vasodilation, pain, fever, platelet aggregation). They are not the primary drivers of the switch to *pro-resolving* mediators. The vignette specifically highlights the shift *away* from pro-inflammatory mediators like LTB4 and *towards* pro-resolving mediators like LXA4, which is not the role of COX.
- (B) **5-Lipoxygenase (5-LOX):** 5-LOX produces leukotrienes, notably LTB4, which is a potent chemoattractant for neutrophils and a key pro-inflammatory mediator. The vignette shows a *decrease* in LTB4 during resolution, suggesting reduced 5-LOX activity or a shift away from its products. While 15-LOX can interact with the 5-LOX pathway to produce lipoxins, 5-LOX itself is the source of the pro-inflammatory mediator that is decreasing, not the enzyme initiating the switch to resolution.
- (C) **12-Lipoxygenase (12-LOX):** 12-LOX produces 12-HETE. Its main roles are related to platelet function, smooth muscle contraction, and potentially some inflammatory responses, but it is not considered a central player in the switch from pro-inflammatory to pro-resolving lipid mediators during the resolution of inflammation, especially compared to 15-LOX.
- (E) **Phospholipase A2 (PLA2):** PLA2 releases arachidonic acid from cell membranes. This is the *first step* in the synthesis of *all* eicosanoids (pro-inflammatory and pro-resolving). While essential, it is not specific to the *switch* mechanism described in the vignette. The question asks for the enzyme initiating the *switch*, implying a more specific downstream event.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- **High-Yield Takeaway:** The resolution phase of inflammation is characterized by a switch from pro-inflammatory mediators (e

<a id='question-040'></a>

### Question 40: Vascular Physiology - Endothelium-Derived Hyperpolarizing Factor (EDHF)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Arachidonic acid metabolism via cytochrome P450 epoxygenases produces epoxyeicosatrienoic acids (EETs), which act as endothelium-derived hyperpolarizing factors (EDHF) causing vasodilation by opening calcium-activated potassium channels (KCa) in vascular smooth muscle. (花生四烯酸通过细胞色素P450环氧化酶代谢产生环氧二十碳三烯酸 (EETs)，作为内皮来源的超极化因子 (EDHF) 导致血管舒张，通过打开血管平滑肌中的钙激活钾通道 (KCa) 实现。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old male with a history of hypertension and hyperlipidemia presents to the emergency department complaining of severe, sudden-onset chest pain radiating to his left arm. His blood pressure is 180/110 mmHg, heart rate is 110 bpm, and respiratory rate is 24 breaths/min. An electrocardiogram (ECG) shows ST-segment elevation in leads II, III, and aVF, consistent with an acute inferior myocardial infarction. The patient is immediately taken to the cardiac catheterization laboratory. Coronary angiography reveals a complete occlusion of the right coronary artery (RCA). Following successful percutaneous coronary intervention (PCI) with stent placement, the patient's chest pain resolves, and his ECG normalizes. However, despite the restored blood flow, the patient continues to experience significant angina pectoris with minimal exertion. Further investigation reveals intact endothelial function in the treated RCA segment, as assessed by acetylcholine-induced vasodilation. However, when the RCA is perfused with a solution containing a specific inhibitor of cytochrome P450 epoxygenase, the acetylcholine-induced vasodilation is significantly blunted. Which of the following mechanisms is most likely responsible for the residual angina in this patient, despite restored coronary blood flow and intact acetylcholine-induced vasodilation?

(A) Impaired nitric oxide synthase (NOS) activity leading to reduced production of nitric oxide (NO), a primary endothelium-derived relaxing factor (EDRF).
(B) Decreased activity of cyclooxygenase (COX) enzymes, resulting in reduced production of prostacyclin (PGI2), another EDRF.
(C) Reduced production of epoxyeicosatrienoic acids (EETs) by cytochrome P450 epoxygenases, impairing the endothelium-derived hyperpolarizing factor (EDHF) pathway.
(D) Increased sensitivity of vascular smooth muscle cells to vasoconstrictors, such as endothelin-1 (ET-1), due to endothelial dysfunction.
(E) Upregulation of lipoxygenase (LOX) activity, leading to excessive production of leukotrienes, which cause vasoconstriction.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 62M, HTN, HLD, acute inferior MI (RCA occlusion), successful PCI. This sets the stage for coronary artery disease and intervention.
- **Residual Angina:** Despite PCI restoring blood flow and intact acetylcholine-induced vasodilation, the patient still has angina. This suggests a problem beyond simple mechanical obstruction.
- **Intact Acetylcholine Vasodilation:** Acetylcholine primarily stimulates endothelial NOS to produce NO, a major EDRF. Intact response implies NO pathway is functional.
- **Blunted Vasodilation with CYP Inhibitor:** Inhibition of cytochrome P450 epoxygenase specifically impairs the EET pathway. The fact that this blunts the acetylcholine response (which is already intact) indicates that EETs contribute significantly to the overall acetylcholine-induced vasodilation in this patient's coronary arteries.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the mechanism responsible for residual angina despite restored flow and intact NO-mediated vasodilation.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **Arachidonic Acid Metabolism:** Arachidonic acid is metabolized via three main pathways: cyclooxygenase (COX) producing prostaglandins and thromboxanes, lipoxygenase (LOX) producing leukotrienes, and cytochrome P450 epoxygenases producing EETs.
- **Endothelium-Derived Factors:** The endothelium releases several factors affecting vascular tone, including NO (EDRF), PGI2 (another EDRF), EETs (EDHF), and endothelin-1 (vasoconstrictor).
- **Acetylcholine Action:** Acetylcholine stimulates endothelial cells to release NO via endothelial NOS (eNOS). NO diffuses to vascular smooth muscle cells, activates guanylate cyclase, increases cGMP, leading to smooth muscle relaxation and vasodilation.
- **EETs as EDHF:** EETs are produced by CYP epoxygenases (CYP2C and CYP2J families) in endothelial cells. They act as EDHF, contributing to vasodilation, particularly in situations where NO signaling is impaired or requires augmentation. EETs activate KCa channels (e.g., KCa2.3, KCa3.1) in vascular smooth muscle cells. Opening these channels leads to K+ efflux, hyperpolarization of the cell membrane, inactivation of voltage-gated Ca2+ channels, reduced intracellular Ca2+, and ultimately smooth muscle relaxation and vasodilation.
- **Clinical Scenario Interpretation:** The patient has coronary artery disease. While PCI restored mechanical patency, endothelial function might be subtly impaired or require multiple pathways for optimal vasodilation. The intact acetylcholine response confirms the NO pathway is working. However, the blunting of acetylcholine response by a CYP inhibitor demonstrates that the EET pathway (EDHF) is also active and contributes significantly to the overall vasodilation stimulated by acetylcholine. The residual angina suggests that the full vasodilatory capacity needed to meet myocardial oxygen demand is not achieved, likely due to impaired EET production or signaling. Therefore, reduced EET production is the most plausible explanation for the persistent symptoms despite restored flow and intact NO function.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Impaired NOS activity would lead to reduced NO production. This would cause endothelial dysfunction, typically resulting in a *reduced* acetylcholine-induced vasodilation, which contradicts the finding of intact acetylcholine response. This pathway is likely intact based on the vignette.
- (B): Decreased COX activity would reduce PGI2 production. PGI2 is another vasodilator. While endothelial dysfunction can affect PGI2, the intact acetylcholine response (primarily NO-mediated) and the specific blunting by a CYP inhibitor point away from COX being the primary issue for the *residual* angina in this specific context.
- (D): Increased sensitivity to ET-1 would cause vasoconstriction. While endothelial dysfunction can involve altered ET-1 levels or sensitivity, the primary finding is *impaired vasodilation* despite restored flow, and the specific CYP inhibitor effect points towards a problem with EET-mediated vasodilation, not increased vasoconstriction sensitivity. Endothelial dysfunction often involves an imbalance between vasodilators (NO, PGI2, EETs) and vasoconstrictors (ET-1), but the vignette focuses on the vasodilatory pathways.
- (E): Upregulation of LOX activity leading to excessive leukotrienes would cause vasoconstriction. Leukotrienes are primarily involved in inflammation and bronchoconstriction, not typically the primary mediators of coronary vasodilation or the cause of angina in this setting. This mechanism is inconsistent with the clinical picture and the specific experimental finding involving a CYP inhibitor.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- **High-Yield Takeaway:** Understand the three major pathways of arachidonic acid metabolism (COX, LOX, CYP epoxygenases) and the key vasoactive products (prostaglandins/thromboxanes, leukotrienes, EETs). Recognize EETs as endothelium-derived hyperpolarizing factors (EDHF) acting via KCa channels to cause vasodilation, distinct from the nitric oxide (EDRF) pathway. Appreciate that multiple pathways contribute to endothelial function and vasodilation.
- **Differentials:** Consider other causes of endothelial dysfunction (e.g., oxidative stress, inflammation, shear stress), impaired NO bioavailability (e.g., scavenging by reactive oxygen species), or microvascular dysfunction. However, the specific experimental manipulation (
